#3181. Signal to restrict screenshots over AI agent concerns – again

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

The messaging app similarly limited screenshots because of Microsoft Recall last year.

Also: My 5 favorite Siri AI ‘onscreen awareness’ tricks – and why

Signal also blocked screenshots last year in response to Microsoft’s controversial Recall AI feature, which could capture screenshots of Signal chats on devices running Windows 11.

Also: How I use Meta Muse safely to stretch my AI budget – and what I never let it do

Just last week, researchers at Google DeepMind used AI agents to formally verify Signal’s code, building on Signal’s own work to essentially prove the app’s encryption works. That’s a high bar for a security guarantee within the app, but it becomes meaningless if an agent can simply grab messages off a screen.

Samsung is reportedly working on its own agent, Galaxy Claw, and Apple is developing a rival for Claude and ChatGPT as well.

Whittaker noted Signal’s screenshot restriction isn’t perfect, saying that AI agents can still access app data through other means, “such as retrieving content already present on a user’s device via the filesystem.” Earlier this week, a Reuters investigation supported by Signal and the AI Now Institute found that Meta’s Muse AI agent was using permissions from its initial setup in unexpected ways to access apps and data that a user had not explicitly connected it to.

Signal said its protections don’t currently work on MacOS.

If you’d still like to take screenshots, you can simply disable the setting, though Signal’s announcement strongly advised against it.

#3180. Google Is Killing the Nest App. Here’s What Comes Next

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Don't panic: All your devices should transfer over to Google Home fairly seamlessly.

Google Home is replacing Nest and bringing new devices.

If you’re a Nest smart home user, a major change is coming in early 2027 that will affect how you manage your smart home. Google has officially confirmed to CNET via email that both the Nest app and Google Assistant are being sunset, and current users will lose access to both next year.

All Nest devices will lose Nest app support in favor of the Google Home app, and the Nest app will be retired on Jan. 25, 2027. Google Assistant will be removed from home devices on Aug. 2, 2027, with all support moving to the Gemini for Home voice assistant.

The change is part of Google’s effort to consolidate its various apps and devices into a single app and voice experience. In a statement sent to CNET, Google confirmed that “every device supported today works with these updated experiences.”

Nest Thermostats should all seamlessly port over to Google Home.

Google has confirmed that Google Home will support all your Nest devices, including thermostats, cameras, doorbells, smoke detectors and locks. Existing schedules and settings should transfer over automatically, which is often a hassle when you switch between apps and devices. You should also be able to pair your Nest devices with any Works with Google Home and Matter smart home devices, as well as Google Home speakers and smart displays and TV streamers, all with the same app.

As part of the switchover, you should be able to access Nest devices on various platforms outside of the mobile app. It’ll support iOS and Android widgets, the Home Panel on Android, smart displays, speakers and the Google Home web page. That includes being able to check live camera views, adjust your thermostat and lock your front door, right from a TV or smartwatch.

Google Assistant may be leaving, but Google is releasing newer, more powerful devices.

However, to continue controlling and receiving notifications from your Nest devices remotely, you will need to switch to the Google Home app or home.google.com if you prefer to control them via a web browser.

You can change Google voice assistant voices, but not the wake words.

On Aug. 2, 2027, Gemini for Home voice assistant will fully replace Google Assistant on speakers and smart displays. The switch to Gemini makes a lot of sense if you’ve used Google Assistant at all. Assistant is very much an older voice assistant, lacking the smarter follow-ups and natural-language capabilities that make Gemini more useful.With Gemini, you should get answers to more complex questions, and you can speak more naturally without barking commands, letting it handle conversation context and remember multiple interactions.

Getting ready for Google Home is easy, though Google has an entire Nest App Transition Support Hub if you need step-by-step instructions to walk you through everything that’s needed.

For most people, it should be a simple matter of downloading the Google Home app. Naturally, a Google Account is required, but as mentioned, your Nest devices, including the cameras and video history, will automatically transfer from the Nest app starting on Jan. 25, 2027.

Gemini’s Google Home integrations are useful, but command options can include some risks.

The other thing you may have to do is download any custom video clips you’ve created on home.nest.com. Manually saved clips won’t transfer to the Google Home app along with your video history. To make sure these are backed up, you’ll want to visit home.nest.com before Jan. 25, 2027, and use the Download All button to save clips you want to keep.

The final deadline to migrate your Nest account to a Google account before the company starts data deletions March 25, 2027.

Google says Nest devices, including cameras and video history, will automatically transfer from the Nest app to the Google Home app. Nest Aware (1st Gen) subscriptions should also automatically switch to Google Home Premium so that coverage continues without interruption. Google Home Premium is replacing Nest Aware (1st Gen), and users will receive an email closer to the transfer date detailing your subscription information.

Google’s smart displays should all work perfectly with the various Nest devices.

Gemini for Home is already available in many countries and languages. Google announced earlier that it’s expanding to more regions over the coming months. Users in a supported region can upgrade to Gemini Home right now.

On your phone, open the latest version of the Google Home app, tap your profile icon in the top right to open Home Settings, then tap “Get Started” on the “Set up Gemini for Home” banner. On your computer, just visit home.google.com/gemini.

#3179. Peapack-Gladstone: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Flat expenses amid expanding lending reveal how disciplined cost control is converting revenue growth into higher returns.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 20, 2026 at 4:45PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Peapack-Gladstone Financial ›

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Oct. 28, 2026, Peapack-Gladstone (PGC -1.50%) reported core pre-tax pre-provision revenue (earnings before taxes and credit loss reserves) of $32.3 million, up 76% year over year. Expanding operating margins and disciplined expense management indicate that multiyear investments in talent and branding are generating higher core returns.

Management offers three key takeaways on how this strategic momentum is taking shape.

The bank is generating positive operating leverage, a condition where revenue increases faster than operating expenses, through strict cost control. Management kept overhead flat across recent quarters while expanding core lending and wealth management activities.

Management is actively reducing exposure to its New York rent-regulated multifamily portfolio, which consists of commercial loans on apartment buildings subject to local rent control limits. Allowing these loans to run off mitigates credit risk while freeing up lending capacity for higher-yielding commercial relationships.

To maintain consistent performance, executive leadership measures quarterly progress against an internal hurdle known as the 2-2-2 framework. This operational strategy sets explicit targets for expanding the balance sheet and growing core earnings every three months.

Business volume is expanding across multiple commercial lending categories, and executive leadership is displaying clear satisfaction with the operational trajectory. The franchise maintains structural advantages through its private banking model, deep wealth management relationships, and expanding footprint in Metro New York. Executive leadership is watching deposit pricing discipline and net interest margin (the spread between interest earned on assets and paid on liabilities) as interest rates fluctuate.

Long-term shareholder value creation hinges on converting recent top-line momentum into higher return benchmarks across changing rate environments. Management remains committed to delivering long-term profitability targets that reflect full execution of its private banking business model.

#3178. Mortgage Rates Today, Thursday, November 19: Kind of a Big Jump

TL;DR: Mortgage rates rose enough today that you might be feeling some sticker shock.

If you're here looking for lower mortgage interest rates, today is definitely not your day.

The average interest rate on a 30-year, fixed-rate mortgage jumped to 7.38% APR, according to rates provided to NerdWallet by Zillow. This is 25 basis points higher than yesterday and 12 basis points higher than a week ago. (A basis point is one one-hundredth of a percentage point.)

Keep in mind that mortgage rates are always on the move, and that if you're tracking rates day-to-day, you're going to see a lot of volatility. Zooming out and looking at the bigger picture — like a graph showing at least a month's worth of rate data — can help you see the overall trend.

Here are the average APRs we're seeing today, as well as movement over the past month for context:

» Take the next step: Compare mortgage rates from NerdWallet’s top lenders

Mortgage rates are constantly changing, since a major part of how rates are set depends on reactions to new inflation reports, job numbers, Fed meetings, global news ... you name it. For example, even tiny changes in the bond market can shift mortgage pricing.

On Friday, the Bureau of Labor Statistics released the Producer Price Index for October, which tracks the change in prices that U.S. producers are able to get for their products. This is one way to measure inflation on the market end, rather than the consumer end.

The results were mixed. While prices didn’t rise quite as high as economists anticipated month-over-month (0.3% vs. 0.4%), year-over-year price growth did reach 6.5% — the largest increase in four years. This points to even higher costs for consumers coming in the near future.

Investors are convinced that the Federal Reserve will raise overnight borrowing rates again in December to pull inflation down. And higher overnight borrowing rates — called the federal funds rate, which is what banks pay to fund products like mortgages — usually lead to higher mortgage rates.

» MORE: How the Federal Reserve affects mortgage rates

Refinancing might make sense if today’s rates are at least 0.5 to 0.75 of a percentage point lower than your current rate (and if you plan to stay in your home long enough to break even on closing costs).

With rates where they are right now, you may want to start considering a refi if your current rate is around 7.88% or higher — but there aren’t many people for whom that’s true.

Also consider your goals: Are you trying to lower your monthly payment, shorten your loan term or turn home equity into cash? For example, you might be more comfortable with paying a higher rate for a cash-out refinance than you would for a rate-and-term refinance, so long as the overall costs are lower than if you kept your original mortgage and added a HELOC or home equity loan.

If you're looking for a lower rate, use NerdWallet's refinance calculator to estimate savings and understand how long it would take to break even on the costs of refinancing.

» Time to refi? Compare refinance rates from NerdWallet’s top lenders

There is no universal “right” time to start shopping — what matters is whether you can comfortably afford a mortgage now at today’s rates.

If the answer is yes, don’t get too hung up on whether you could be missing out on lower rates later; you can refinance down the road. Focus on getting preapproved, comparing lender offers, and understanding what monthly payment works for your budget.

NerdWallet’s affordability calculator can help you estimate your potential monthly payment. If a new home isn’t in the cards right now, there are still things you can do to strengthen your buyer profile. Take this time to pay down existing debts and build your down payment savings. Not only will this free up more cash flow for a future mortgage payment, it can also get you a better interest rate when you’re ready to buy.

» Is now a good time to buy? See NerdWallet’s analysis

If you already have a quote you’re happy with, you should consider locking your mortgage rate, especially if your lender offers a float-down option. A float-down lets you take advantage of a better rate if the market drops during your lock period.

Rate locks protect you from increases while your loan is processed, and with the market forever bouncing around, that peace of mind can be worth it.

🤓 Nerdy Reminder: Rates can change daily, and even hourly. If you’re happy with the deal you have, it’s okay to commit.

» Stay informed: Check out NerdWallet's mortgage news hub for all our latest coverage.

The rate you see advertised is a sample rate — usually for a borrower with perfect credit, making a big down payment, and paying for mortgage points. That won't match every buyer's circumstances.

#3177. Even the Iconic Google Home Page Can’t Survive the Push for More AI

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

The search giant is revamping Google.com with AI options.

This is not your parents’ Google home page. In its push to get more users to adopt its AI tools, the search engine giant is rolling out a raft of new features worldwide.

The new elements include three new buttons below the search bar at Google.com: Create images, Ask about files and Brainstorm. The Google Search button is gone, but the I’m Feeling Lucky button remains.

The buttons are available when using Google on a desktop but not on mobile. I was able to see the new buttons when signed in to my Google account.

When signed out, I was only able to see the typical home page, with the only AI button being AI Mode, which has been there since May 2025.

Clicking on the “Create images” button opens up a new page where you can create an AI-generated image using Google’s Nano Banana. It’s the same image-generation tool you can use on other Google platforms such as the Gemini app, Google AI Studio and AI Mode in Search.

Clicking “Ask about files” allows for several options. You can have the AI analyze a document and rewrite it, simply have it answer questions or ask it to perform tasks such as “polish my resume” or “draft an email with action items.”

The “Brainstorm” button prompts you to “describe your goal,” such as making a to-do list, learning how to code or finding a table at a restaurant.

Another new feature, as announced by Google Search Product VP Robby Stein on X, is a collapsible sidebar on the left side of the home page, which shows your recent AI search threads and offers the ability to create a new one:

Stein said the new sidebar will help users go back to where they left off.

“People are coming to Search with more questions that aren’t just one and done — and in testing, this sidebar made a big difference for making progress & continuing to explore the web,” Stein posted.

I didn’t see the collapsible sidebar during my testing of the home page.

Google has integrated its Gemini AI models throughout its various platforms — Search, Gmail, Docs, Pixel phones and third-party apps such as Adobe, Shopify, OpenTable and Instacart. This past week, Google announced several other new AI features — integrations with stores such as Albertsons, Safeway and Target to complete orders and connect to apps such as Peloton and Adobe.

Over the summer, Google’s Gemini app topped 1 billion users in what CEO Sundar Pichai called the company’s “fastest growing product ever.”

But Google’s rush to force-feed AI into its products has met with plenty of pushback. The New York City, Los Angeles and Chicago school districts said they might choose different search engines because Google said it will not remove AI from search tools used by students, The New York Times reported.

Google said search and the Gemini app will be turned off for schools unless school administrators want them. But Google said there will not be a chatbot-free version of search available for schools. So schools declining AI will not be able to search using Google at all.

Google’s AI Overviews, which appear at the top of many search page results, have also been blamed for a steep drop in web traffic to content publishers. AI Overviews summarize user search queries and replace the long list of blue hyperlinks that once appeared across search pages. So instead of clicking on those links to get more information, giving publishers valuable clicks, a user can simply get their query response by reading the AI Overview.

#3176. Asked on Reddit: I Lost My Job; What Should I Do First?

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

So how do we make money? Our partners compensate us for advertisements that appear on our site. This compensation helps us provide tools and services - like free credit score access and monitoring. With the exception of mortgage, home equity and other home-lending products or services, partner compensation is one of several factors that may affect which products we highlight and where they appear on our site. Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

Income loss is stressful; the first steps involve cutting costs and focusing on essentials.

A Redditor recently shared that they were struggling to decide which financial steps to take first after a job loss.

Should they withdraw money from retirement savings to have more cash on hand — but pay an early withdrawal penalty? Or is it better to rely on the severance payout to handle expenses?

Many users responded with good ideas: File for unemployment, try to avoid tapping into retirement savings, and cut expenses as much as possible.

For more ideas, I asked three financial experts how to manage money in the immediate aftermath of job loss.

While it’s tempting to make quick decisions about tapping into a retirement fund or selling a house, it’s better to wait on any big moves, says Parker Wine, retirement planning advisor at the financial firm Daniel A. White & Associates in Lewes, Delaware.

That’s because you need time to digest the situation and weigh the pros and cons of different options, he says. “People can get impulsive and panic, and it’s usually a bad thing to make decisions when you’re panicking,” he says.

Take your time, get second opinions and think through all of your options first, Wine suggests.

If you’re fortunate enough to receive a severance package, wait to sign it until you’ve reviewed it carefully and understand all of the details, says John Rafferty, partner and investment advisor representative at Solomon Financial in Carmel, Indiana.

For example, make sure you know about your health insurance options, such as whether you’ll receive insurance through COBRA or need to take out a new policy in the health insurance marketplace.

Filing for unemployment and checking into any other available workplace benefits — such as help with your new job search — is also an important part of understanding what support to expect in the weeks and months ahead, Rafferty adds.

Creating a bare-bones budget stripped of “extras” like restaurant meals or travel can help the severance payment last as long as possible, Rafferty says.

“Now that you don’t have income coming in, ask yourself, ‘How much can I spend and where?’”

You might be able to cut back on entertainment costs, coffee runs and clothing purchases, for example.

At the same time, it’s important to prioritize the essentials like mortgage payments, rent, utilities and food. Rafferty recommends calling your mortgage company, utilities provider and any other lenders to ask if they have a hardship program for those experiencing financial challenges.

Opting in could give you more time to pay your bills or temporarily lower payments.

It’s hard to stick to a budget that eliminates all entertainment, so Rafferty suggests finding enjoyable activities that come with a low price tag. Hiking, discovering a new park and participating in free community events are all ways to enjoy downtime on a budget.

“Having that release is mentally a big thing so you can stay prepared for the job search,” he says. “Otherwise, it’s easy to get down and feel negative, and we need things to help us feel positive.”

Similarly, connecting with friends and family during this time is crucial, says Laura Adams, accredited financial counselor and financial coach in Wilmington, North Carolina.

People often feel a sense of shame or embarrassment after job loss, and Adams encourages people to talk about their feelings and ask for help if needed. “Financial shame can cause us to make emotional rather than confident financial decisions,” she says.

Starting some kind of side hustle to bring in extra cash can also help bridge the gap between the job loss and your next job, Wine says. Substitute teaching, working at a local public golf course and driving for a rideshare service are all options he’s seen people try.

Doing something new also helps stave off boredom while unemployed, but he cautions that you’ll still owe taxes on any side income you bring in, so plan accordingly.

All three financial experts also emphasized that a job loss is easier to handle with an emergency savings fund you can turn to, and suggested building one up — ideally three to six months’ worth of expenses. But starting small helps, too — while employed, if possible.

The Redditor who posted the question mentioned that, like many Americans, they lacked emergency savings. This added urgency to the question of what to prioritize.

Reddit is an online forum where users share their thoughts in “threads” on various topics. The popular site includes plenty of discussion on financial subjects like how to deal with job loss, so we sifted through Reddit forums to get a pulse check. People post anonymously, so we cannot confirm their individual experiences or circumstances.

If you’ve been outside, at the gym, or on a basketball court, there’s a good chance you’ve seen someone wearing Reeboks. The brand has had its fair share of ups and downs, but it’s riding a wave of increased interest driven by smart business moves, such as a refocus on sports and lifestyle, alongside high-profile collaborations like the Reebok G-Unit G6 50 Cent.

Founded in 1958, Reebok has changed hands and gone through multiple iterations. Most recently, in 2022, Adidas sold Reebok to Authentic Brands Group for over two billion dollars. #3173. With Mortgage Rates Rising, Should You Hibernate Until Spring?

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

So how do we make money? Our partners compensate us for advertisements that appear on our site. This compensation helps us provide tools and services - like free credit score access and monitoring. With the exception of mortgage, home equity and other home-lending products or services, partner compensation is one of several factors that may affect which products we highlight and where they appear on our site. Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

Higher borrowing costs aren't the whole story. Here's what to consider before putting your house hunt on hold.

Mortgage rates are back up this week, wiping out modest relief from a monthlong drop that began in mid-October.

The average rate on a 30-year fixed-rate mortgage shot up 29 basis points to 7.28% APR in the week ending Nov. 19, according to rates provided to NerdWallet by Zillow. (A basis point is one one-hundredth of a percentage point.) We calculate our weekly average using daily APRs recorded over the past five business days.

A mortgage is a big ol’ loan, so when rates go up, those fractions of a percentage point add up fast. Say you’re borrowing $400,000 on a 30-year fixed-rate mortgage. Compared with last week, your monthly principal and interest payment would be about $78 higher today.

One bad week doesn't make a trend, but a jump like this can make putting your house hunt on hold until spring feel like a safer bet. So … is it?

Before deciding whether to buy a house now or wait, it helps to know what's pushing mortgage rates higher.

Right now, mortgage rates are still feeling the aftershocks from two major inflation reports last week. First, the Consumer Price Index (CPI) showed consumer prices rose more than expected in October. Then, the Producer Price Index (PPI) showed wholesale inflation climbed 6.5% over the past year — its biggest annual increase in nearly four years.

While CPI shows how prices have changed, PPI can offer clues about what's coming next. Faced with higher costs, businesses may raise their own prices to compensate, keeping inflation elevated. Federal Reserve policymakers aren't expecting much relief anytime soon, either. According to meeting minutes released Nov. 18, they expect inflation to remain elevated above their 2% target for a while.

To cool rising prices, the Fed can raise the federal funds rate, which is the benchmark interest rate banks charge each other for overnight loans. The Fed doesn't set mortgage rates, but lenders anticipate its decisions, so mortgage rates often move before the Fed acts. A rate hike is widely expected at the Fed's Dec. 8-9 meeting.

Even after a Fed rate hike, there’s no telling how quickly inflation will cool — or whether mortgage rates will follow. (Inflation is just one of many forces that affect mortgage rates, after all.) So if you do wait until spring to buy a house, don't do it just because you're betting on a cheaper mortgage.

If you're not in a rush to move, winter can be a good time to regroup. Fewer homes typically go up for sale from November through February, so waiting until spring could give you more options.

But new data from the National Association of Realtors suggests this year's slowdown goes beyond the usual seasonal lull. High mortgage rates and home prices are making it tough for many buyers to make the numbers work. For some buyers, waiting isn't a strategy — it's the only option.

Pending home sales, a measure of homes under contract, fell 16.7% in October compared with a year earlier, NAR reports. All four U.S. regions saw declines. At this rate, NAR chief economist Lawrence Yun warned that annual home sales could fall to their lowest level since the mid-1990s.

“Either sizable housing demand is building in the background, or the dream of homeownership is increasingly out of reach for many Americans,” Yun said in a news release.

Whether you're waiting by choice or necessity, you can use the time to strengthen your finances. Paying down debt, strengthening your credit score or saving for a bigger down payment could help you qualify for a better mortgage deal.

Every little bit helps when today’s mortgage rates are this high.

Mortgage preapprovals typically expire after 60 to 90 days. If you're already preapproved, why not keep an eye on listings this winter? (Hey, I’m an optimist. All it takes is one house.)

Think like a seller for a second. If you're listing your house in the dead of winter, you're probably not doing it just for fun. You might have a deadline, and you’re probably more willing to negotiate. In the winter, buyers have less competition — and more room to negotiate a lower price, closing cost credits or repairs. Those savings on the sticker price might help offset today's higher borrowing costs.

Spring typically brings more homes for sale, but it also brings more buyers competing for them. And if mortgage rates end up dropping this spring, you won't be the only one coming out of hibernation.

#3170. Crypto Market Today, Nov. 20: Bitcoin Plunges Below $60,000 as Wall Street's Sell-Off Spreads to Crypto

A wave of selling across financial markets is pushing cryptocurrencies toward fresh bear market lows, raising concerns about how much further digital asset prices could fall.

By RJ Fulton – Nov 20, 2026 at 4:45PM EST

Cryptocurrency markets suffered another brutal sell-off on Friday, November 20, as a wave of selling across Wall Street spilled into digital assets. Bitcoin broke below the psychologically important $60,000 level, while Ethereum and Solana experienced even steeper declines as investors continued to retreat from riskier assets.

As of early evening, Bitcoin (BTC -9.65%) had plunged 6.01% to $58,186.39, while Ethereum (ETH -9.95%) tumbled 9.03% to $1,596.72 and Solana (SOL -9.33%) declined 8.02% to $67.21. The latest losses come as major cryptocurrencies struggle to establish a bottom, with weakening liquidity and persistent selling pressure raising the prospect of fresh bear market lows.

Deteriorating investor risk appetite continued to weigh on cryptocurrencies Friday as concerns over interest rates, elevated valuations, and tighter financial conditions fueled a broad sell-off across global markets. With the Nasdaq plunging more than 5%, investors appeared increasingly focused on raising cash and reducing exposure to riskier assets.

The simultaneous decline in stocks, gold, and cryptocurrencies suggests selling pressure is extending beyond individual markets. Rather than simply rotating capital between asset classes, investors appear to be reducing their overall risk exposure, leaving cryptocurrencies particularly vulnerable as liquidity conditions continue to deteriorate.

Spot cryptocurrency ETFs suffered steep losses on Friday, mirroring the broader sell-off in digital assets. The iShares Bitcoin Trust ETF (IBIT -5.87%) fell 5.87%, while the Fidelity Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC -6.16%) declined 5.97%. Meanwhile, the iShares Ethereum Trust ETF (ETHA -9.16%) plunged 9.16%, reflecting Ethereum's particularly sharp decline.

Bitcoin's break below $60,000 marks another significant deterioration in the cryptocurrency market's technical outlook. With Ethereum approaching $1,500 and Solana falling below $70, several major cryptocurrencies are now trading near levels that could determine whether the current bear market extends into 2027. If selling pressure persists, investors could see another round of new lows before a sustainable recovery takes shape.

Beyond price action, the broader withdrawal of capital from digital assets presents a more fundamental challenge. Bitcoin ETF outflows have reportedly exceeded $2.5 billion in 2026, while the simultaneous sell-off across stocks, gold, and cryptocurrencies suggests investors are prioritizing liquidity over risk-taking. Without renewed capital inflows, even sharp relief rallies may struggle to develop into sustained recoveries.

Looking ahead, investors should watch whether Bitcoin can reclaim $60,000, Ethereum can recover above $1,800, and Solana can establish support above $70. Improving ETF flows, stabilizing prices, and a recovery in broader financial markets would provide more convincing evidence that conditions are improving. Until then, the combination of weakening demand and unfavorable macroeconomic conditions leaves cryptocurrencies vulnerable to further declines heading into year-end.

#3169. What Security Teams Still Get Wrong About Asset Visibility

Yochai Corem is GM of Exposure Management at Check Point Software.

Organizations have more asset data than ever, but turning that data into a reliable view of what they own, operate and expose remains difficult.

A server appears in three tools with different identifiers. A cloud workload has an owner who has left the business. A forgotten website remains online after the campaign that funded it has ended. Each leaves security teams with information they still have to investigate before they can act.

Security leaders should judge asset visibility by how reliably it supports a decision. A useful inventory helps teams establish what an asset does, who is responsible for it, how it is protected and what should happen when a problem appears.

Several familiar assumptions prevent organizations from reaching that standard.

An incomplete configuration management database (CMDB) should prompt a review of how records are created, updated and retired.

Start by defining its purpose. The CMDB should record the systems, services and dependencies needed to manage business operations, with accountable owners and lifecycle status. Detailed security telemetry can remain in specialist systems, linked through reliable identifiers. An impersonating website belongs in an external threat register connected to the affected brand.

Then identify which sources can reveal missing records. Cloud platforms, endpoint management, identity providers, vulnerability scanners and procurement systems can each expose inconsistencies. Buying a service should trigger registration; retiring it should trigger checks that access, infrastructure and records have been removed.

Reconciliation should reflect the pace of change. I would set a daily reconciliation target for cloud and endpoint feeds; use deployment and retirement events to trigger updates; and review unresolved discrepancies weekly. Quarterly ownership reviews can supplement those checks.

An asset without an owner needs an interim accountable team and an escalation deadline. “Unknown” should describe an investigation in progress.

The modern attack surface spans internal infrastructure, cloud workloads, software as a service (SaaS), internet-facing systems, supplier connections and the organization’s broader digital presence.

Those categories need different forms of accountability. Infrastructure teams may manage servers, cloud teams manage workloads and business application owners sponsor SaaS services. Marketing or regional teams may control domains and social accounts. Supplier relationships require a business sponsor alongside technical oversight.

Security should establish a review and response process across those boundaries. A newly discovered domain needs ownership verification and an assessment of what it hosts. An unfamiliar SaaS service needs checks on its users, data and access. A fraudulent social account needs investigation and a disruption process.

Review frequency should follow exposure and change, with continuous discovery where practical and scheduled reviews for ownership, supplier access and dormant services. This broader scope aligns with the National Institute of Standards and Technology’s (NIST) inclusion of hardware, software, services and supplier services in asset management.

Leaders can test this assumption by following a newly discovered asset through the security process.

Does discovery trigger checks for required endpoint protection, vulnerability scanning and an accountable owner? Does an unmanaged internet-facing system reach someone authorized to restrict or remove it? Can the organization confirm that the resulting action occurred?

These outcomes show whether visibility operates as a security control. The Center for Internet Security’s (CIS) Control 1 connects inventory management with identifying and addressing unauthorized or unmanaged assets.

Useful measures include the time taken to assign ownership, the proportion of applicable assets covered by required controls and the age of unresolved discrepancies. A growing inventory count provides little reassurance when newly discovered systems remain unmanaged.

Additional tools introduce additional records, each with its own identifiers, timestamps and assumptions.

Effective reconciliation requires explicit rules. Stable cloud resource identifiers or device identifiers should take precedence over an Internet Protocol (IP) address that may change or be reused. Records need source and freshness information so teams can distinguish a current observation from a stale entry.

Organizations also need rules for conflicting fields. The identity system may establish who uses a device, while a service registry identifies the team accountable for the application it supports. Those relationships should remain distinguishable.

Cyber asset attack surface management (CAASM) can support this work. Organizations can also improve CMDB integrations, use cloud asset inventories and service catalogs, or build reconciliation workflows in their existing data platforms. The operational requirements remain consistent: reliable matching, clear authority for each field and a process for resolving exceptions.

Discovery should initiate a decision about management, protection or retirement.

A forgotten website needs an owner to confirm whether it still serves a business purpose. A vulnerable application needs assessment of its exposure, dependencies and operational constraints before a response is selected.

That decision should remain traceable through completion. If a service is retired, verify that it is no longer reachable. If a security control is applied, confirm that it covers the intended asset. Updating the inventory should accompany the operational change.

This ongoing connection between asset information and action also underpins continuous threat exposure management (CTEM).

CTEM depends on this discipline throughout scoping, discovery, prioritization, validation and mobilization.

An unknown asset cannot enter the prioritization process. A known asset without business context is difficult to rank. Missing dependencies weaken assessments of attack paths and change impact. An unknown owner delays action, while stale records undermine confirmation that an exposure has been reduced.

CAASM and external attack surface management (EASM) can support these stages by reconciling internal records and discovering internet-facing assets. Their value depends on the ownership, decision rules and response processes surrounding them.

Security leaders should expect asset visibility to connect discovery with accountable action. Every significant asset needs enough current context for someone to determine its protection requirements, address its exposures and verify the result. That is how a reliable inventory helps reduce the opportunities available to an attacker.​

Forbes Technology Council is an invitation-only community for world-class CIOs, CTOs and technology executives. Do I qualify?

#3167. SpaceX Plans to Spend $2.8 Billion on Gas Turbines. Here’s What That Much Natural Gas Demand Means for Pipeline Investors.

Natural gas will remain in high demand as the data center market expands.

When SpaceX (SPCX -7.69%) went public in June, it disclosed $2.8 billion in purchase commitments for gas turbines and related equipment in its IPO prospectus. SpaceX is purchasing those turbines to power its own data centers and factories.

That massive investment in behind-the-meter (BTM) natural gas connections, which generate electricity on the customer's side of the electric meter rather than going through an energy provider, is a bright green flag for the natural gas market. U.S. data centers already generate over 40% of their electricity from natural gas, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). That percentage could continue to rise as the cloud and AI markets expand.

An easy way to profit from that secular trend is through midstream pipeline companies, which deliver the natural gas to those data centers. Pipeline operators charge companies "tolls" to use their infrastructure, so they're well-insulated from volatile commodity prices. Instead, they simply need the resources to keep flowing through their pipes to generate stable profits. Most pipeline companies also return lots of cash to their investors through high dividends and distributions.

As demand for natural gas rises, pipeline companies will need to expand their infrastructure to handle the increased volumes. Those investments could compress their near-term margins, but they'll support their long-term growth. Let's see which of these pipeline operators will benefit the most from the rapid growth of the cloud infrastructure and AI data center markets.

The Williams Companies (WMB -2.75%) operates more than 32,000 miles of pipeline across 24 states. It owns Transco, the largest natural gas pipeline in the U.S., which connects the Gulf Coast to the Atlantic seaboard. Its pipelines handle roughly a third of all natural gas produced in the United States.

Unlike more diversified pipeline companies, Williams generates most of its revenue by gathering, processing, storing, and transporting natural gas. A smaller percentage of revenue comes from natural gas liquids (NGLs), crude oil handling services, and electricity generation.

From 2025 to 2028, analysts expect Williams' EPS to grow at a 12% CAGR. It still looks reasonably valued at 26 times next year's earnings, and it could command an even higher valuation if the data center market heats up again.

Williams has raised its dividend annually over the past nine years, and it's paid continuous dividends for the past 52 years. It currently pays a forward yield of 3.2%, and its low payout ratio of 82% gives it ample room for future dividend hikes.

Energy Transfer (ET -2.62%) operates more than 140,000 miles of pipeline across 44 states. It transports crude oil, natural gas, natural gas liquids (NGLs), and other refined products through its pipelines. It also exports NGLs through its marine export terminals.

Energy Transfer's pipelines transport about 30% of the natural gas produced in the United States. It generates only approximately 40% of its adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) from natural gas, which makes it less of a direct play on natural gas than Williams. Still, Energy Transfer is more exposed to that market than many of its peers.

Unlike Williams, which operates as a traditional corporation, Energy Transfer is a master limited partnership (MLP) that treats its investors as partners rather than shareholders. MLPs pay distributions -- much of which are classified as a "return of capital" for more tax-efficient returns -- rather than traditional dividends. That strategy enables it to pay a high forward yield of 7.2%, but its investors must file an additional K-1 form (for MLPs) with their taxes each year.

From 2025 to 2028, analysts expect Energy Transfer's earnings per unit (EPU) to grow at a 13% CAGR. It trades at just 12 times next's year's EPU, and that low valuation should limit its downside even if the cloud and AI markets cool off over the next few years.

#3164. This Oceanfront Hilton Resort in Florida Is Great for Groups

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

The recently renovated Signia by Hilton Diplomat Beach Resort offers great views, a well-equipped fitness center and delicious food.

Hilton is making a big play in South Florida with its Signia by Hilton Diplomat Beach Resort. The massive 1,000-room property — which underwent an $80 million renovation before opening under the Signia Hilton brand in May 2026 — sits on the beach in Hollywood, Florida, just outside of Fort Lauderdale.

The resort caters primarily to business and group travelers (it’s attached to a massive events space), but it’s also a strong option for vacationers looking for some R&R. The main downside: The location isn’t great if your trip primarily revolves around off-property excursions, dining and nightlife.

Room rates typically run $300 to $600 a night, depending on the season, but you can also use Hilton Honors benefits to make your stay more affordable. There’s also a resort fee of $59 per night — but Hilton waives that when you book using Hilton points.

Hilton covered the cost of my stay for two nights, but the opinions in this review are my own. Here’s what guests can expect at this oceanfront resort.

Since the Signia by Hilton Diplomat Beach Resort is part of Hilton Honors, there are several ways to book a stay there.

If you’re using Hilton points, expect to pay 70,000 to 80,000 points a night when standard rooms are available. Hilton elite status — a benefit you can earn by holding any Hilton Honors credit card — gets you the fifth-night-free perk, where you stay for at least five nights but pay for four when booking with points.

For example, if you were to stay five nights at 80,000 points a night, you’d pay 320,000 points total, bringing down your average nightly rate to 64,000 points.

You can also book using Hilton’s generous free night certificates, which can be used at almost any property in the portfolio. If you hold the Hilton Honors American Express Aspire Card, you automatically get a certificate each year. It’s also possible to earn certificates through reaching spending thresholds on the card and on the Hilton Honors American Express Surpass® Card. Terms apply.

Make sure to check the cash rate against the points rate to make sure you’re getting good value from your redemption. NerdWallet values Hilton points at 0.4 cent apiece.

For cash bookings of two nights or more, you might consider using The Hotel Collection via American Express’ travel portal for eligible cardholders. Eligible bookings come with benefits like a $100 property credit and other amenities subject to availability, including a room upgrade, early check-in and late checkout.

Keep in mind that third-party bookings typically don’t earn Hilton points or count toward elite status qualification.

It’s hard to miss the Signia by Hilton Diplomat Beach Resort, with its signature H-shaped facade and sleek white-and-glass exterior.

Stepping into the hotel, I was immediately struck by the lush greenery filling the entrance and indoor courtyard. The grand lobby has soaring glass ceilings, giving the space a greenhouse feel, although the strong air conditioning keeps you cool.

Despite its massive size, it doesn’t have a sprawling layout, which I appreciated. Everything is close together, and you can walk from the check-in counter to any of the indoor restaurants in under a minute.

I stayed in an oceanfront suite with a balcony, which was spacious and had a fun beachy vibe. To get to the rooms, you’ll need to use either the north or south tower elevator banks.

In the living room, there was a large sitting area featuring a TV, a couch, an accent chair and a four-person high-top table.

Although I didn’t spend too much time on the balcony — the October balminess was a bit too much for me — I enjoyed stepping out each morning to admire the view.

The bedroom had a king bed with extremely comfortable bedding and an oversized chair that was a great spot to relax and read a book by the window.

The bathroom was spacious, featuring a shower and a separate tub (something every room has).

Even the standard room I toured felt roomy. I liked that it shared similar elements with the suite, including the oversized lounge chair.

This is what you’d normally get when booking a room at the cheapest points price.

There are several dining venues on the property, ranging from a grab-and-go cafe to a high-end steakhouse. It is a luxury resort, so expect the prices to reflect that.

For breakfast, you can head to Palmea Kitchen, which is also open for lunch. I had the breakfast buffet, which offered a nice spread of classics, from hot food items and pastries to cold cuts and smoked salmon.

If you don’t have time for a sit-down breakfast, you can head next door to The Press, which offers made-to-order and grab-and-go food options throughout the day. You can also get smoothies, juices, snacks, pastries and other sweet treats.

The Canteen is another grab-and-go option that also sells snacks, drinks, souvenirs and sundries, similar to a convenience store.

For lunch and dinner, try Solara. It doesn’t have a traditional restaurant feel, as it’s mostly an indoor-outdoor bar space. One side connects to the lobby, while the other side faces the Solara adults-only pool.

It serves a lunch menu, as well as a substantial bar food menu that’s available late into the night.

You can also eat at Playa Beach Bar & Grill, a beachside restaurant serving Latin-inspired cuisine. I enjoyed spending a lazy lunch there and watching the waves crash onto the shore.

For a fancy sit-down dinner, go to Diplomat Prime, the property’s steakhouse. We had a group dinner at the restaurant, and the meats were, of course, the highlight. But don’t sleep on the seafood, which was also delicious.

One of the best parts of the Dip, as it’s affectionately known, is its location right on the beach. Guests can access the stretch of beach directly adjacent to the property. But it’s not a private beach, so you might encounter crowds.

I didn’t spend any time on the beach itself — it was extremely windy while I was there — but the hotel’s resort fee includes amenities like a beach lounge (umbrella not included), a daily one-hour kayak or paddleboard rental, and beachfront yoga classes.

If you don’t want to get sand everywhere, you can head to one of the three pool options: the adults-only Solara pool, the family-friendly lagoon pool and the Verandah Social Club for those 21 and older (requires paid access).

The family-friendly lagoon spans the length of the property and runs beneath the Solara pool. It also has its own hot tub.

I spent an afternoon in a cabana at the Verandah Social Club, which has some smaller plunge pools and a hot tub.

I appreciated the attentive service from the staff there, who constantly checked on whether guests needed anything.

If you’re looking for other ways to stay active, head to the two-story fitness center, featuring a variety of equipment like free weights, exercise bikes, a rowing machine and treadmills.

Next door, you’ll find the spa and wellness center. I had a relaxing massage that incorporated hot stones and aromatherapy.

The locker rooms come with a sauna and steam room, although I wished there were more amenities like a hot tub and cold plunge.

One of the resort fee inclusions is complimentary high-intensity interval training workout classes at the gym across the street. I signed up for an early morning class. The instructor was friendly, and it was a great way to start my day.

Other amenities include a complimentary one-hour e-bike rental each day, as well as a kids’ club with various activities (but drop-off fees apply).

I had a fantastic stay at this resort, but I’d recommend it for a specific traveler.

If you’re planning to stay on-property your entire trip, it’s a great option. That’s probably why it’s such a popular convention hotel, since everything you need is easily accessible.

I loved the hotel’s design, including the jungle-like atmosphere in the lobby, and I found the dining options to be tasty, albeit pricey.

But if your trip revolves around visiting Miami attractions, you’ll likely want to stay closer to the action.

#3162. Credo Technology Group vs. Western Digital: Which Technology Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

Credo's AI connectivity and Western Digital's storage rebound are very different bets, and the valuation gap between them may tip the scales.

By Sara Appino – Updated Nov 20, 2026 at 4:57PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Credo Technology Group ›

The race for faster data processing is pushing infrastructure to its limits, forcing a choice between specialized connectivity and massive storage. You might be deciding between Credo Technology Group (CRDO -15.20%) and Western Digital (WDC -12.02%) for 2027.

Credo provides high-speed connectivity solutions that enable artificial intelligence data centers to communicate efficiently, while Western Digital is a global leader in both hard disk and flash storage. While they operate in different niches, both are essential players in the scaling of cloud infrastructure. This comparison evaluates which stock offers the best path for long-term growth.

Credo Technology Group specializes in high-speed copper and optical interconnect solutions designed for artificial intelligence infrastructure. The company sells products like Active Electrical Cables and optical components to hyperscale operators and system vendors. It has established key partnerships with Oracle (ORCL -8.48%) and Microsoft (MSFT -2.77%) to enhance connectivity for dual-architecture systems.

The demand for faster semiconductor stocks has significantly boosted its financial performance. In the 12 months ended Aug. 1, 2026, revenue reached nearly $1.6 billion, representing a 165.1% increase over the previous period. This expansion led to a net income of roughly $538.3 million and a net margin of about 33.8%.

As of its May 2026 balance sheet, the debt-to-equity ratio is 0.0x, meaning the company carries no debt relative to shareholder equity. Its current ratio is approximately 10.2x, indicating a strong ability to cover short-term debts with current assets. In the fiscal year ended May 2, 2026, free cash flow reached nearly $407.0 million, though stock-based compensation represented roughly 39.3% of operating cash flow, which inflates reported cash generation since SBC is a non-cash expense added back in the cash flow statement.

Western Digital designs and supplies essential data-storage solutions for enterprises and hyperscale cloud providers. Its product portfolio includes both hard disk drives and flash-based storage serving a global market across Asia and the Americas. Customer concentration like this adds a layer of risk, with three individual clients accounting for 16%, 15%, and 13% of total net revenue in its latest annual report.

In the 12 months ended Oct. 2, 2026, revenue reached approximately $14.5 billion, representing a 43% increase compared to the prior year. Net income for this period was nearly $10.0 billion, which indicates a net margin of roughly 69.2%. This performance suggests a robust recovery in cloud storage demand, which now accounts for nearly 89% of its net revenue.

On its July 2026 balance sheet, the company shows a debt-to-equity ratio of nearly 0.1x, meaning total debt is low relative to equity. The current ratio stands at approximately 1.3x, measuring how well the company meets its short-term financial obligations. In the fiscal year ended July 3, 2026, free cash flow reached nearly $3.5 billion, representing operating cash flow minus capital expenditures.

Credo Technology Group faces intense competition from larger industry peers like Broadcom (AVGO -12.19%), Marvell Technology (MRVL -11.69%), and Astera Labs (ALAB -14.77%). The company also deals with geopolitical risks due to its reliance on Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company for all semiconductor manufacturing. Furthermore, it manages previously flagged intellectual property litigation with Marvell Technology, which was recently resolved via a settlement.

Western Digital must navigate risks associated with its complex strategic initiatives, such as the 2025 separation of its former flash business unit. It competes directly with other storage manufacturers like Seagate Technology Holdings (STX -12.35%) and providers of alternative storage technologies. Additionally, the company relies on a limited supply base for critical components, leaving it exposed to potential global supply chain disruptions.

Western Digital is cheaper, trading at a lower Forward P/E for future earnings estimates and a lower P/S ratio based on sales over the past twelve months.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

I'd go with Credo Technology, a company whose sales have more than doubled year over year for seven straight quarters. AI data centers run on thousands of chips that need to share data quickly, and Credo is a key player that makes the connections between them.

Management expects sales to grow more than 85% this fiscal year, and Credo is also very profitable, turning about half of its sales into adjusted profit. It's also moving into optical products that carry data farther than its copper cables can, and management expects them to bring in more than $600 million this fiscal year. The stock fell after its recent earnings report, as investors worried that a few big customers make up most of its sales, but Credo added another big customer in its latest quarter.

To its credit, Western Digital's hard drives store much of the data AI creates, and its profit has jumped. But rival Toshiba is reportedly doubling its hard drive capacity, which could pull prices down. For long-term investors, Credo pairs fast growth with strong profits, and it has a front-row seat as AI data centers get bigger. With plenty of cash on hand, it can keep investing in new products for years to come.

#3161. How To Teach Your AI Agent About Your Company

Frederik Riskær Pedersen, Founder and CEO of Maplith, a company brain for your AI agents.

For 16 years, I ran a translation company that grew from a handful of people into an international business. The most important thing I learned about knowledge is that it rarely lives where you think it does.

One customer shows it well. We had to fetch their files from their own system and deliver them in their own layout, and we could only invoice some of the repetitions. None of that was written down anywhere. It lived with one project manager. Anyone else would have done it the standard way and got it wrong.

That tailoring was not a side issue. It was the product. If the work had been standard, the customer could have run the model themselves. This is why a company’s rules never get standardized away: The exceptions are often exactly what customers pay for.

I think of this every time someone asks me why their AI agent isn’t delivering. The models are good enough. The problem is that the agent starts from zero every time. It does not know which systems you run, how they connect or which rules you follow without ever writing them down. So it guesses, and a confident guess in your accounts is worse than no answer at all.

Here are five things you can do this month before you let an agent work inside your business. None of them needs a new tool.

Book two hours with the three or four people who make most of the everyday decisions. Ask them one question: “What would a new hire ask you in their first week?” The answers are your unwritten rules: which customers get special terms, when a refund is approved, what can be invoiced and what cannot. Write each one as a short sentence, specific enough to check: “This customer gets files back in their own template.” “Refunds above 300 euros are approved by the finance lead.”

List every system you use and what each one is the source of truth for. You will find places where two systems describe the same thing differently. “Returned” in the ERP can mean the customer has sent the item back, while in the warehouse it means the item is back on the shelf.

The instinct is to make the systems agree. In my experience, that rarely works because each system was set up for the team that uses it, and that is why it works for them. Write the translation down instead. The same goes for numbers. In my company, the price did not sit in the same place from one customer to the next. Note where it lives for whom and which value to trust for which question.

Most useful answers need two systems joined: the order in the web shop and the invoice in accounting, or the support ticket and the customer in the CRM. Find the field that ties them together and test it by hand. Take 20 random orders and look for them in the other system. If you find 19, you can build on that link. If you find 12, you cannot, and an agent will rarely tell you it is guessing. The test takes an afternoon and saves months of wrong reports.

Split actions into four levels: read, propose, act after approval and act alone. Start with everything that changes something requiring approval from a named person. Set limits in money and volume, not in trust. When the agent has proposed the same action a hundred times without a mistake, move that one action up a level. That decision should be yours, never the agent’s.

For every action, record who asked for it, who approved it, what happened and when. It sounds bureaucratic, but it is the only way to answer when a customer, an auditor or your board asks, “Why did this happen?” Without a log, you also cannot tell when it is safe to give the agent more freedom.

None of this is about artificial intelligence. It is about making knowledge that lives in people’s heads, and in systems that do not talk to each other, available and trustworthy. Companies that do this get more out of their AI. They also become less fragile when a key person goes on holiday or leaves.

So start small. Book the two hours this week, write down 10 rules and test one link.

Forbes Technology Council is an invitation-only community for world-class CIOs, CTOs and technology executives. Do I qualify?

Atmos Rewards offers six communities, each with its own travel perks and opportunities to earn bonus points

Atmos Rewards is the airline loyalty program for Alaska Airlines and Hawaiian Airlines. One of its newer features is Atmos Communities, which offer additional rewards and benefits tailored to different travel interests.

There are six communities, including two reserved for Alaska and Hawaii residents. The other four are open to members regardless of where they live. But don’t get caught up in the community names. The best choice for you may depend more on your travel plans than the groupings suggest.

Here’s how to decide which Atmos Community best fits your travel patterns.

Atmos Communities are like groups within the Atmos Rewards program that offer additional benefits based on different travel interests.

They’re free to join for Atmos Rewards members. Four communities are available to all members, while community Club 49 is reserved for Alaska residents and community Huaka’i by Hawaiian is reserved for Hawaii residents.

You can belong to only one community at a time and switch communities once per calendar year. Consider your upcoming travel plans before choosing.

Joining a community is easy: Log in to your Atmos Rewards account and navigate to “Account overview.” Select “Join a Community,” browse the options and click the “Join” button under the one you choose.

Each Atmos Community has a list of 10 destinations where members can earn bonus points and status points. Although the destinations differ by community, the rewards structure is the same.

Through Dec. 31, 2027, members can earn up to 10,000 bonus points and 10,000 status points by visiting qualifying destinations.

Here’s how many points you’ll earn as you reach each milestone:

Visit one destination, earn 100 status and bonus points.

Visit two destinations, earn 200 status and bonus points.

Visit three destinations, earn 300 status and bonus points.

Visit four destinations, earn 400 status and bonus points.

Visit five destinations, earn 500 status and bonus points.

Visit six destinations, earn 750 status and bonus points.

Visit seven destinations, earn 1,000 status and bonus points.

Visit eight destinations, earn 1,250 status and bonus points.

Visit nine destinations, earn 2,500 status and bonus points.

Visit 10 destinations, earn 3,000 status and bonus points.

The first member of each community to visit all 10 qualifying destinations during the challenge period will also earn 100,000 bonus points.

New lists of qualifying destinations for each community take effect on Jan. 1, 2028.

Here’s a closer look at the six communities, their benefits and the destinations eligible for bonus points.

Global Locals is geared toward frequent international travelers. Benefits include:

Earn 10% more status points toward Atmos elite status on flights that begin and/or end outside the U.S., including award flights.

Earn 1 status point for every 25 points transferred to Atmos Rewards from Bilt or Marriott Bonvoy.

Get 20% off Atmos Rewards Unlocked experiences.

Through Dec. 31, 2027, these 10 Oneworld Alliance hubs count toward bonus points and status points. Qualifying layovers also count toward the destination challenge, so you don’t necessarily need to make these cities your final stop.

If you frequently travel internationally or have any upcoming flights through any of these airports, including qualifying layovers, Global Locals might be a fit for you.

If you have the Atmos™ Rewards Summit Visa Infinite® credit card, you’ll earn 3 Atmos points per $1 spent on eligible Alaska and Hawaiian Airlines purchases, dining and foreign transactions. The last bonus category can be especially useful for frequent international travelers.

This community has a similar setup to Global Locals, but most of its bonus destinations are in the U.S. Here’s what you can get:

Get 20% off Atmos Rewards Unlocked experiences.

Through Dec. 31, 2027, here are the 10 destinations that count toward the bonus:

This community would be an excellent choice if your upcoming travels include several of these airports, even if dining isn't the main reason for your trips. If dining out is one of your favorite activities, check out the best credit cards for restaurants.

Active Escapes focuses on airports in outdoor-oriented destinations that are all in the U.S., save one. Benefits here are:

Get 20% off Atmos Rewards Unlocked experiences.

Through Dec. 31, 2027, here are the 10 destinations that count toward the bonus:

While these airports are gateways to national parks and other natural attractions, you don’t have to be an outdoor enthusiast to benefit. Like before, don’t let the heading sway you. Rather, consider the destinations before you decide.

Families On the Go might appeal to you if you redeem Atmos Rewards for your children. Benefits include:

Get 20% of your points back on eligible award flights for kids 12 and under after you complete your trip.

Get 20% off Atmos Rewards Unlocked experiences.

Through Dec. 31, 2027, these destinations qualify for bonus points and status points:

This community may be especially valuable if you regularly book award flights for children 12 and under. Even if you don't travel with kids, it could be worth considering if its qualifying airports align better with your travel plans than those of other communities.

Exclusively for Alaska residents, Club 49 offers rewards that are focused on travel to, from and within the state:

Two free checked bags when traveling to or from Alaska on Alaska Airlines or Hawaiian Airlines.

Three free checked bags when traveling within Alaska on Alaska Airlines.

Two Travel Now discounts per year for 30% off one-way refundable Y-class fares on Alaska Airlines, booked within four days of departure for travel to, from or within Alaska.

Ship cargo within Alaska for $49 plus tax (weight, size and content limits apply).

Get 20% off Atmos Rewards Unlocked experiences.

Baggage benefits don’t combine with other free checked bag allowances, such as those that come with some Alaska credit cards. However, they apply to everyone traveling on the same reservation.

Through Dec. 31, 2027, here are the 10 destinations that count toward the bonus offers:

While this community is only for Alaskans, you might decide one of the other communities more closely matches your travel plans — especially if you already have an Alaska credit card with free baggage benefits. However, if you ship a lot of cargo or think you’ll use the Travel Now discounts, those are special benefits that may sway you to choose Club 49.

Hawaii residents have their own community just for them: Huaka’i by Hawaiian. It offers benefits focused on interisland travel:

Two free checked bags on neighbor island flights on Hawaiian Airlines, including sports equipment like surfboards and golf bags.

Quarterly fare discounts of 10% off one neighbor island booking, or 20% off for eligible Hawaiian Airlines® World Elite Mastercard® and Atmos™ Rewards Visa® cardholders.

Earn 50% more points and status points on flights between Hawaiian islands beginning Jan. 1, 2027.

Get 20% off Atmos Rewards Unlocked experiences.

The 10 destinations eligible for bonus points through Dec. 31, 2027 are:

Seven destinations are in Hawaii, with one on the mainland and two international cities. If you live in Hawaii and like to hop between islands, this community is a strong choice.

Don’t be persuaded by the names of the communities. Look a little deeper, because there’s a lot of overlap between destination lists. Compare the options against your upcoming travel plans.

You can change communities once per calendar year, so you aren’t locked in to your initial choice indefinitely. However, switching communities resets your destination-challenge progress, but you’ll retain any points you’ve already earned.

If your home airport (or one you visit frequently) is already on a community’s list, you may have an easier time earning points. Also consider whether a particular community benefit, such as free checked bags or points back on award flights for kids, outweighs the destination challenge.

Finally, pay attention to airport codes. For example, Tokyo’s Narita (NRT) and Haneda (HND) appear on different community lists. New York’s qualifying airport is JFK, while it is DCA in Washington, D.C. If you have a choice between airports, check which one qualifies for your community’s challenge.

#3159. Trust Is The New Battleground For Customer Engagement

James Garvert is the EVP of Fraud & Communications Solutions for TransUnion.

​Engaging with consumers has never been more essential, or more difficult. Amid the meteoric rise of AI-enabled scams, call spoofing and social engineering attacks, the challenges organizations face when trying to reach consumers seem insurmountable.

Every interaction depends on trust, yet consumers are more hesitant than ever to engage. Many of the traditional warning signs consumers were trained to recognize have disappeared—consumers are on edge.

And with good reason: It’s a jungle out there.

In our recent global study, “61% of business leaders ranked fraud among the risks with the greatest potential business impact, ahead of cybersecurity breaches, compliance, customer churn, operational cost and reputational risk. More concerning, 58% said they’re very or extremely concerned fraud is evolving faster than their organizations’ abilities to detect and prevent it.”

Historically, organizations have viewed fraud and engaging with customers as separate disciplines. But today’s threat environment makes it clear they’re deeply interconnected. In many cases, the communication channel itself—whether a phone call, text message, email or mobile app interaction—provides the first indication that fraud may be occurring.

Consumers don’t experience fraud, identity and communication challenges as separate issues. To them, it’s one experience, one problem. It’s a trust problem that’s reshaping the customer journey. In fact, 69% of consumers say they would switch vendors for stronger security.

Trust has become the foundation for both customer engagement and fraud prevention.

At our recent Fraud & Communications Summit in Chicago, keynote speaker Matt O’Neill, a former U.S. Secret Service Special Agent and recipient of Gold and Silver Awards for distinguished service, shared insights from years spent working undercover as a banker, broker, money launderer and other personas. In these roles, he led multinational investigations into crypto-laundering networks and dark web marketplaces.

According to O’Neill, fraudsters are winning because fraud has evolved into a highly organized industry. He explained that scam operations now function much like large multinational corporations. Scam compounds are similar to call centers, complete with organizational structures, performance quotas, shift schedules and human resources departments. Unfortunately, many of these compounds operate under sophisticated criminal networks and employ workers who have been trafficked.

At the same time, the barrier to entry into the fraud world has never been lower. AI enables fraudsters to create convincing scams at scale in virtually any language, including cloned voices, deepfake videos and personalized social engineering campaigns. And it’s cheap.​

The consequences of eroding trust extend well beyond fraud losses. Organizations often experience:

Ironically, the industries and brands most trusted by consumers are frequently among the most targeted by fraudsters, with the highest fraud instances related to auto lending, bank-issued and retail credit cards and unsecured personal lending.

According to Edelman, trust now rivals price and quality as a primary factor influencing consumer decision-making. Research shows that “77% of consumers cited confidence their personal data is secure as the most important feature when choosing whom to transact with online.”

Organizations can strengthen trust by approaching fraud prevention and customer communications as a unified strategy.

Traditional fraud controls were designed for predictable attacks. Today’s fraudsters adapt quickly, vary their tactics and coordinate attacks across multiple channels and sessions. Identity verification remains important, but it only confirms who someone claims to be. It doesn’t determine whether the current interaction should be trusted.

Here’s where identity verification falls short:

• KYC (Know Your Customer) controls are often strongest at onboarding, not throughout the customer lifecycle.

• Devices, behaviors and risk profiles change over time.

• Credentials can be compromised after verification.

• Synthetic identities can appear legitimate on paper.

The most important fraud signal is often not a single event. Any single signal can be manipulated or generate false positives. It’s the story that unfolds throughout an interaction. It emerges through relationships among users, devices, accounts, entities and sessions.

As if it wasn’t hard enough, AI has turbocharged fraud. In addition, labeling identified threats as AI-driven remains a challenge. That’s because it’s difficult to build comprehensive strategies with new AI fraud models evolving so rapidly.

While fraud practitioners know when an account was taken over, there’s currently no label for how it was taken over and whether AI drove the attack. As a result, the AI-attributed training set doesn’t exist yet for tracking that fraud, and detection stays anchored to heuristics and analyst judgment.

To be able to engage effectively with consumers and prevent fraud, organizations must adopt continuous authentication and layered risk strategies.

• Develop a comprehensive strategy—don’t address fraud in silos across your organization.

• Form strategic partnerships with your cyber teams.

• Correlate identity, device, behavioral and contextual signals in real time.

The process is straightforward: Collect. Correlate. Score. Decide.

Every phone call, text message, email and digital transaction represents a trust moment. Customers want confidence the organization contacting them is legitimate and their information and transactions are protected. Simply asking consumers to “trust us” is no longer enough.

Verified identity, authenticated communications and real-time risk intelligence are becoming the foundation of trust in a digital-first world.

The future isn’t reactive fraud mitigation. It’s proactive trust at the point of interaction, by orchestrating identity, risk and communications intelligence across every customer interaction.

Forbes Technology Council is an invitation-only community for world-class CIOs, CTOs and technology executives. Do I qualify?
