#3064. The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats!

The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats! originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

This 76ers team is a heck of a lot of fun to watch and fun basketball means fun stats!

At 13-3, the 76ers are exactly a month into the 5th-best start in franchise history and best since they were 14-2 in 2000-01. Which turned out to be a pretty good year. They were 15-1 in 1949-50 and 1966-67 and 14-2 in 1981-82.

To help everybody get through this interminable two-day gap between 76ers games, here's our first 2026-27 installment of Roob's 76ers Stats, with a tip of the hat to Stathead.

→ The 76ers have been out-rebounded by 34 through 16 games (719-685) and their 13-3 record matches the best in NBA history by a team that's been out-rebounded after 16 games. Three other teams have been 13-3 despite getting out-rebounded, most recently the 2013-14 Miami Heat. The others are the 1979-80 Bucks and 2012-2013 Spurs. The 76ers are an NBA-best 6-3 this year when they've been out-rebounded (which means they're 7-0 when they have a rebounding edge).

→ In the win over Orlando Thursday, V.J. Edgecombe had 24 points, eight rebounds, six assists and two steals, and Joel Embiid had 26 points, six rebounds, seven assists and two steals. It was the first time two 76ers had at least 24 points, six rebounds and assists and two steals in a game in 53 years. In a win over the Celtics at the Spectrum in 1973, Fred Carter had 31 points, six rebounds, six assists and seven steals and George McGinniss had 28 points, 16 rebounds, eight assists and three steals.

→ Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, a career 37 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 44 percent so far. Anfernee Simons, a career 38 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 41 percent. Jabari Walker, a career 33 percent 3-point shooter, is at 42 percent. Dean Wade, a career 37 percent shooter, is at 41 percent. Jaylen Brown, a career 36 percent shooter from range, is at 39 percent. The 76ers are shooting just 36 percent as a team from the stripe because James and Embiid have been so bad, but everybody else is off to career-best starts from 3-point range.

→ The 76ers are 7-1 on the road, equalling their best road record ever after eight games. They were also 7-1 in 1980-81, 1981-82 and 1982-83. The 1981 team had the best road start in franchise history, going 12-1 before losing its second road game.

→ With 15.7 points, 6.8 assists and 6.8 rebounds per game, LeBron James is only the fifth 76er ever to average at least 15 ½ points and 6 ½ assists and rebounds through 16 games. The others are some familiar names: Wilt Chamberlain in 1967, Charles Barkley in 1987, Ben Simmons in 2018 and James Harden in 2023.

→ The 76ers already have more wins this year after trailing by 10 points through the first quarter than all but two full seasons since 1950. They had four in 1985-86 and again in 2022-23. The Hawks also have three wins this year when trailing by double digits after the first quarter, but they're 3-4 and the 76ers are 3-2. So they actually have a winning record when they're down by double digits going into the second quarter.

→ With Jaylen Brown (22.6, 6.6), Joel Embiid (20.0, 8.3), LeBron James (15.7, 6.8) and V.J. Edgecombe (13.6, 6.5), the 76ers are the only NBA team with four players averaging 6 ½ rebounds and 13 points. The last time they had four players in a season who averaged 13 points and 6 ½ rebounds was 1961-62 when they were the Syracuse Nationals, with Dave Gambee (16.7, 7.9), Hal Greer (22.8, 7.4), Red Kerr (16.3, 14.7), Dolph Schayes (14.7, 7.8) and Lee Shaffer (16.9, 6.8).

→ Edgecombe's 6.5 rebounds per game are 2nd-most among shooting guards behind only the Grizzlies' Cedric Coward, who is at 8.5. Edgecombe's 6.5 is also 2nd-highest ever by a 76ers shooting guard behind only Greer's 7.4 during that 1961-62 season. Edgecombe averaged 5.6 rebounds as a rookie and his 5.8 career average is highest by a shooting guard who spent more than one year with the 76ers. Greer is second at 5.3. Among all NBA players who've played at least 90 games, Edgecombe's 5.8 average is 11th-highest all-time among shooting guards (although Stathead includes some swing guard-forwards in their data).

→ Joel Embiid's 14.6 shooting percentage from 3 is worst in NBA history after 16 games (minimum 40 attempts). Embiid is 6-for-41 from the stripe this year. The previous 3-point shooting low 16 games into a season belonged to Cedric Ceballos of the Lakers in 1995-96 and the 76ers' Evan Turner in 2013-14. Both were 6-for-43 for 16.3 percent.

→ LeBron James is at 22.2 percent from 3 on 10-for-45. The Sixers are the first NBA team in 12 years with two players under 23 percent after 16 games with a minimum of 40 attempts. In 2014 Tony Wroten was 10-for-50 (10 percent) and Turner was 6-for-43 (16.3 percent).

→ Baca artikel asli

#3063. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 stocks for the long haul

Global stock markets have been volatile as investors assess high bond yields and continued tensions between the U.S. and Iran. There are concerns about the impact of uncertainty in the Middle East on oil prices and consequently on inflation.

Investors who prefer to look beyond short-term noise and buy stocks with attractive long-term growth potential can follow the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts. The ratings of these experts are backed by in-depth research of a company's financials and thorough analysis of the factors affecting its business performance.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Semiconductor giant Nvidia (NVDA) reported stellar fiscal second-quarter results and reassured investors about continued strength in AI-driven demand for its chips. Moreover, Nvidia's FY28 revenue growth outlook of 70% was well above the Street's expectations.

Reacting to the Q2 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reiterated a buy rating on NVDA and increased his price target to $300 from $288. "This is our Top Pick in the semis group, with a compelling product cycle, exceptional growth and valuation below peers," said the 5-star analyst.

Nvidia delivered impressive quarterly results despite supply constraints, Moore said, but he believes that the FY28 revenue and gross margin commentary mattered more, with a favorable outlook for both measures.

Moore said Nvidia's 70% revenue growth guidance for FY28 was above his own estimate of 52% and the consensus of about 40%. The top-line growth estimate was a "remarkable figure" given supply issues and Moore said he expects Nvidia to continue to address bottlenecks to deliver rapid growth.

Among other takeaways, the analyst said performance metrics for Vera Rubin chips were strong, with Nvidia emphasizing a 30x higher throughput per megawatt and 35x lower token cost compared with Grace Blackwell Ultra.

Moore ranks No. 159 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 24.60%. See Nvidia Statistics on TipRanks.

Ride-hailing and delivery platform Uber Technologies (UBER) was recommended by BMO Capital analyst Brian Pitz, who discussed the company's opportunity in the autonomous vehicle (AV) market in a recent research note that reiterated a buy rating and price target of $119.

The 5-star analyst highlighted Uber's transition into a diversified marketplace, with investors now focusing on its AV growth prospects. The key question is how much value can Uber generate as AVs become commercialized, Pitz asked.

"We continue to believe that Uber's AV strategy is evolving and will become a significant driver of revenue and profitability," the analyst said.

Importantly, Pitz believes that Uber can establish itself as a preferred mobility platform for AV makers, supported by its growing AV infrastructure capabilities and an expanding partner base. This would enable Uber to win a significant share of value as the AV market is commercialized.

Additionally, Pitz noted Uber's enhanced position in the AV value chain, with the company shifting from serving as a distribution channel for robotaxis to becoming a broader platform for AV mobility. The analyst discussed Uber's efforts to capture growth opportunities in the AV market, including higher capital spending, expansion into new markets and building partnerships beyond Alphabet's Waymo.

Pitz ranks No. 574 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of about 12.20%. See Uber Technologies Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Semiconductor company Marvell Technology (MRVL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the second quarter of FY27. But the company's increased FY28 guidance fell short of investors' high expectations.

In reaction to the Q2 FY27 results, KeyBanc analyst John Vinh reaffirmed a buy rating on Marvell Technology, with a price target of $400. The analyst noted that Marvell's results and outlook were driven by strength in its data center business. The Q2 FY27 data center business grew 46% year-over-year, to $2.17 billion, far above KeyBanc's estimate of $2.09 billion.

Management "highlighted accelerating AI networking demand across scale-out, scale-across and scale-up architectures, supported by strong 800G optical DSP demand, rapid 1.6T ramp, broader 51.2T switch deployments and higher TIA/driver demand," Vinh said.

Consequently, Marvell raised its data center revenue guidance, now expecting 60% growth in FY27 and more than 60% in FY28.

Regarding the recently announced $120 billion warrant arrangement with Google, Vinh said management argued that programs tied to the warrant are already priced into the custom outlook through FY28. Meanwhile, new programs and those in execution are expected to drive significant upside to the previous FY29 custom XPU revenue estimate of over $10 billion, Vinh said, citing management.

Vinh ranks No. 123 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 28.50%. See Marvell Technology Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#3061. 49ers’ optimism grows for Mike Evans’ return in massive Vikings game

The 49ers might have one of their best offensive players back after a lengthy absence Sunday night against the Vikings in Mexico City.

Receiver Mike Evans was expected to be a limited participant in practice for the second day in a row on Friday and listed as questionable, 49ers coach Kyle Shanahan told reporters, giving him a chance to be available for one of the biggest games of the regular season.

Mike Evans (5) of the San Francisco 49ers runs on the field against the Washington Commanders at Levi's Stadium in Santa Clara, Calif., Oct. 19, 2026. Getty Images

"He had a good day of practice yesterday. As always, he's a little sore today but going in the right direction," Shanahan said.

Evans has missed San Francisco's last three games after leaving near the end of the first half of the Oct. 19 win over the Commanders with Achilles tendinitis. Yet despite missing the future Hall of Famer, the 49ers have scored 38 and 46 in their last two games in wins over the Raiders and Cowboys, respectively.

Shanahan was asked what kind of boost Evans would give the offense if he could return against the Vikings' vaunted defense that enters the weekend ranked second in the NFL in EPA per pass attempt and first in quarterback pressure rate, according to Next Gen Stats.

"A big one," Shanahan said. "Having Mike out there, everyone knows how good Mike is. So I think the guys have done a good job of holding it down without him out there. But Mike's our guy and a very unique player, been doing it at a high level for a very long time and played really unbelievable in the games he played in this year. It'd be great to get him back."

Shanahan spoke before practice and noted Evans would be limited after practicing for the first time since his injury on Thursday with the team in Colorado Springs, Colo., preparing for the altitude at Estadio Azteca in Mexico City.

NFL Network's Ian Rapoport said the 49ers are optimistic Evans can play on Sunday night. Though he compared Evans' injury to what running back Christian McCaffrey dealt with in 2024, when he missed the first eight games due to bilateral Achilles tendinitis.

Mike Evans (5) of the San Francisco 49ers runs after a catch against the Washington Commanders in Santa Clara, California, Oct. 19, 2026. Getty Images

"It feels like to me there's a really good chance," Rapoport said on "The Pat McAfee Show." "It's a tough injury. When Christian McCaffrey had it, and obviously, he had it on both feet, both legs. So a little bit different. But I don't think anyone saw McCaffrey being out as long as he did.

"So it's a tough injury [for Evans], and I don't really want to assume because you never know how it's going to react. But there is optimism that he goes this week. And that would be pretty significant. If he could go, and this is an issue that's in the past for him, not something he's going to continue to worry about, then a very, very good receiver joins a very, very good team. I think we were kinda wondering, how would this 49ers' offense be without him? And they looked pretty good. So adding him to the mix would only help."

#3057. Observations after Sixers come back for strong shorthanded victory

Observations after Sixers come back for strong shorthanded victory originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stretched their winning streak to four games and snagged a strong road win Wednesday night over the Magic.

They earned a 121-116 victory in Orlando to improve to 13-3 on the season. The Magic dropped to 6-8.

Joel Embiid had 26 points, seven assists, six rebounds, three blocks and two steals.

VJ Edgecombe posted a season-high 24 points, eight boards and six assists.

LeBron James scored 22 points and Anfernee Simons added 18.

Magic star Franz Wagner had 27 points, 11 assists and eight rebounds.

The Sixers had a long injury list in Orlando. Tyrese Maxey (right foot soreness), Jaylen Brown (left hamstring strain) and Dean Wade (left hamstring soreness/tightness) were among their players out. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse told reporters in Orlando that Brown will be re-evaluated when the team returns to Philadelphia.

Orlando was without star forward Paolo Banchero (right high ankle sprain).

The Sixers will start a three-game homestand when they face the Bucks on Saturday afternoon. Here are observations on their win over the Magic:

The Sixers' undermanned starting lineup was Edgecombe, Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, James and Embiid.

After scoring 37 points in the Sixers' win over the Nets, Embiid had a subpar start. He began 1 for 6 from the floor and committed a turnover that led to an Anthony Black dunk.

The Sixers' lineup looked exactly like a unit that hadn't built any chemistry yet. The team was not sharp early on either end of the floor.

Desmond Bane drained a long three-pointer to give Orlando a 27-12 advantage. Orlando made 11 of its first 14 field goals and led by as many as 17 points in the first quarter.

Labaron Philon Jr. and Justin Edwards joined the Sixers' rotation. So did two-way contract players Tacko Fall and Caleb Love.

Philon impressed in the first quarter, converting two floaters and a corner three. The rookie passed his previous NBA scoring high of five points and finished the night with 10 on 4-for-5 shooting.

Fall played 10 minutes and posted two points, three rebounds and three blocks. He slammed in a dunk and rejected two shots early in the second quarter. Credit to Fall for giving the Sixers a good backup center performance and doing his job as a rim-protecting ultra-big man.

Love entered in the second quarter and was ready to fire, hitting a catch-and-shoot three-pointer on his first touch.

The Sixers' zone defense also helped them eat into the Magic's lead. The zone seemed to disrupt Orlando's offensive rhythm and it was active enough to prevent the Magic from consistently creating high-quality looks.

Edgecombe's buzzer-beating mid-range jumper cut the Sixers' deficit to 69-64 after two quarters.

The Sixers pulled within a single point on multiple occasions in the third quarter, including on a Philon three late in the period. They trailed by five points after the third.

Fall continued to play well early in the fourth quarter. He deterred the Magic's drivers from getting all the way to the rim as the very large back line of the Sixers' zone defense and made eye-catching hustle plays on both sides of the ball.

The Sixers outscored Orlando by seven points on the night with Fall on the court. Soon after Embiid subbed back in, he evened the game at 104-all with two free throws. Jabari Walker swished a three to finally push the Sixers in front.

The Magic regained a late lead with a Jalen Suggs three. James then drove and kicked the ball out to Edgecombe, who nailed one of his customary clutch jumpers to put the Sixers back on top.

No matter the night-to-night personnel, the Sixers have been fantastic at the tail end of close games. They're now 11-0 in clutch situations, which the NBA defines as games within a five-point differential in the final five minutes.

#3050. Top Wall Street analysts recommend these 3 dividend stocks for higher returns

Dividend-paying stocks can enhance portfolio returns by providing a steady income stream to investors and the potential for capital appreciation. Several investors seek exposure to dividend stocks in a volatile backdrop to ensure stable income.

In this regard, recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, backed by strong financials and cash flows to support consistent dividend payments.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Energy Transfer (ET) is a diversified midstream energy company with about 140,000 miles of pipelines and associated energy infrastructure in 44 states. The company made a quarterly cash distribution of 34 cents per common unit for the second quarter of 2026. At an annualized payment of $1.36 per common unit, ET offers a yield of 6.3%.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Jeremy Tonet reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer stock and slightly raised his price target to $25 from $24. The analyst noted that the company delivered a strong Q2 beat across all key metrics.

Tonet highlighted that Energy Transfer raised its 2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance to $18.8 billion-$19.1 billion from $18.2 billion-$18.6 billion, reflecting robust base results and cost optimization. The 5-star analyst added that the company "tightened" its capex to $5.6 billion–$5.9 billion and expects capex above $5 billion annually through 2029, given solid opportunities.

About the Hugh Brinson pipeline, Tonet noted Phase 1 (1.5 bcfd) reaching full capacity by September 1 and Phase II capacity (0.7 bcfd) going online by Q1 2027. The analyst also noted completion of a 14-mile Hugh Brinson lateral in Abilene, Texas, and final stages of talks for an additional 250 mmcfd of Oklahoma power plant demand.

"All in, we see ET well positioned to continue capitalizing on organic growth opportunities across the value chain," said Tonet.

Tonet ranks No. 922 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 9%. See Energy Transfer Financials on TipRanks.

This week's second dividend pick is independent oil and natural gas company Permian Resources (PR). The company declared a base dividend of $0.16 per share for the third quarter, payable on September 30, 2026. At an annualized dividend of $0.64 per share, PR offers a yield of about 2.7%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Permian Resources stock and increased his price target to $27 from $22, citing an improved outlook on oil production growth and operational efficiencies.

The 5-star analyst noted that while PR stock has outperformed its peers so far in 2026, he sees further upside. Mehta is confident about the company's operational improvements and ability to deliver incremental free cash flow per share through the strategic ground game program. He added that the program has completed about $1.05 billion in bolt-on deals this year (as of August 5).

"We see the continued success of PR's ground game as constructive toward future inventory replenishment and increasing working interest over time," said Mehta.

The analyst also emphasized Permian Resources' solid track record of generating growth in free cash flow (FCF) per share. Mehta expects the company's free cash flow per share to grow at a 20% CAGR (compound annual growth rate) from 2025–2028.

Mehta ranks No. 401 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.8%. See Permian Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

Moving on to Sempra Energy (SRE), a utility holding company. Earlier this month, the company declared a quarterly dividend of $0.6575 per share, payable on October 15. At an annualized dividend of $2.63 per share, SRE stock offers a yield of about 3.1%.

Recently, Jefferies analyst Julien Dumoulin-Smith upgraded SRE stock to Buy from Hold but slightly lowered the price target to $97 from $101. The 5-star analyst noted that Sempra stock is trading at a price/earnings multiple that is 14% below its electric peers due to concerns about Texas transmission capital expenditure and the failure of California legislation.

Smith noted that the derating of weaker California peers PG&E (PCG) and Edison International (EIX) is weighing on the sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) valuation but is becoming less relevant given their materially higher fire risk compared to Sempra Energy.

Furthermore, Smith expects Sempra Energy's Texas capex plan to stay in place despite the opposition to transmission and data centers, though delays to 765-kV projects are likely. While a full recovery for SRE stock will need a clearer outcome from the Texas legislative session, Smith believes that the stock may already be an early opportunity for patient investors.

"We are early on the upgrade but see limited downside at current levels as the market is pricing in little transmission upside," said Smith.

Smith ranks No. 945 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.2%. See Sempra ETF Exposure on TipRanks.

#3048. NFL news, live updates: Colts release Daniel Jones, Anthony Richardson Sr. to start at QB; Puka Nacua potentially out for season after knee surgery

We are officially halfway done with the 2026 NFL season, and the NFL is now into Week 11, highlighted by the year's final international game: the Minnesota Vikings take on the San Francisco 49ers in Mexico City, Mexico, for "Sunday Night Football."

The Indianapolis Colts and Houston Texans opened up the week on "Thursday Night Football," but quarterback Daniel Jones continued to struggle, completing just 44.4% of pass attempts for 144 yards and a pick. The Colts followed up with a statement move on Friday, announcing the team would release Jones and promote former No. 4 overall pick Anthony Richardson Sr. to the starting position in a stunning move.

Los Angeles Rams receiver Puka Nacua might be done for the year after undergoing surgery on his MCL on Tuesday. Nacua was already on injured reserve with multiple injuries, but head coach Sean McVay says that he is now poised to miss "a couple months."

That news came one day after Nacua was reportedly suspended three games by the NFL for violating the league's personal conduct policy, as a result of the receiver's series of incidents this offseason; Nacua is serving the suspension while on IR.

Follow along with Yahoo Sports for the latest NFL news, injury updates, and more.

Patriots receiver A.J. Brown is poised to miss significant time: The wideout is expected to be sidelined for multiple weeks after suffering a hastring injury last week, per ESPN's Adam Schefter and Mike Reiss.

Brown has played in just four games this season after sustaining several separate injuries. He was questionable heading into last Sunday's game with ankle and knee issues, but was able to play before suffering the hamstring injury.

The Chiefs will likely be without Kenneth Walker III again for their Week 11 game against the Cardinals, head coach Andy Reid said Friday.

Walker reported groin tightness last week, and the team decided to give him a rest and sit out the 35-13 win over the Atlanta Falcons. Reid said on Wednesday that the running back was "feeling better," but they did not "want to do something stupid."

Through 10 weeks, Walker is fourth in rushing in the NFL with 872 yards and tied for fifth in the league among running backs with seven touchdowns.

Following testing, New England Patriots quarterback Drake Maye reportedly suffered a fractured right fibula in Week 10, per ESPN. Maye sustained the injury after taking a sack on the first drive of Sunday's 35-10 loss to the Lions.

Maye will not require surgery and there is a small chance he could be back for the Patriots late in the regular season if they are contending for an AFC playoff spot.

In a stunning move, Indianapolis has released starting quarterback Daniel Jones. Head coach Shane Steichen announced the news on Friday, adding that Anthony Richardson will be the team's new starting QB going forward.

After being limited in practice on Thursday, Cardinals quarterback Carson Beck will sit out Friday. Head coach Mike LaFleur said the team will prepare veteran Josh Johnson to start this week against the Chiefs.

The 40-year-old Johnson made two starts last season with the Commanders. The Cardinals are his 15th NFL season.

After sitting out practice earlier this week, Buccaneers quarterback Baker Mayfield is heading in the right direction to get back on the field on Sunday.

Mayfield missed Wednesday's practice with injuries to his foot and right thumb, and was limited in practice on Thursday. Though Mayfield's designation for Friday isn't yet available, head coach Todd Bowles told reporters Friday that Mayfield looked good in practice and was trending in the right direction to start.

Mayfield has been dealing with these injuries, but hasn't missed any time. Tampa Bay had a bye last weekend, giving their quarterback a bit more time to rest.

The injury woes of Rams wide receiver Puka Nacua just got more severe: On Friday, head coach Sean McVay announced that Nacua had undergone surgery on his MCL on Tuesday, and that he'll be out for "a couple months." Timeline-wise, that might rule the receiver out for the rest of the season.

Nacua was already on injured reserve with knee and turf toe injuries. He is also serving a three-game suspension for violating the league's personal conduct policy, with the suspension occurring while he's on IR.

Smith left Sunday's game after injuring his ankle. The quarterback has been limited this this week in practice, but will be a full participant on Friday, head coach Aaron Glenn said.

Wide receivers Omar Cooper Jr. (ankle/hip) and Isaiah Williams (ribs) have been ruled out. DT David Onyemata (abdomen), who had his 21-day practice window opened this week, will remain out.

As the 49ers practiced against the backdrop of the Rocky Mountains this week, the Vikings tried to bring Mexico City to the Midwest. They erected a massive altitude chamber at their Eagan practice facility that simulates the low-pressure environment found at elevations as high as 12,000 feet.

Over the past five weeks, Vikings players have subjected themselves to aerobic workouts inside the chamber, using treadmills and stationary bikes to simulate the demands of exercising at altitude. The Vikings hope that altitude training inside their own building reduces the shock factor they experience Sunday night, Tyler Williams, the team's vice president of player health and performance, told reporters in Minnesota on Wednesday.

In an Instagram post, the two-time Pro Bowl defensive end pledged to come back from his injury stronger than ever and to never again jump up to celebrate a big play.

"I honestly can't believe it," Hutchinson wrote in his post. "I've jumped hundreds of times on a football field and never in a million years did I think this would happen to me. But even something like this won't hold me back."

"Two things I know for sure," he added. "1. I always come back stronger. 2. That's the last time I go airborne."

Hutchinson injured his knee on the Lions' first defensive series during their matchup in Munich with the New England Patriots on Sunday.

Reed played 15 of his 16 NFL seasons in Buffalo and was part of those Bills teams that reached four straight Super Bowls. Over 234 career games, he made 951 receptions for 13,198 yards and 87 touchdowns.

The two-time All-Pro and seven-time Pro Bowler was inducted into the Pro Football Hall of Fame in 2014.

Nacua's suspension will go into effect immediately, and he will be eligible for reinstatement on Monday, Dec. 14. The All-Pro wideout is currently on injured reserve with knee and turf toe injuries sustained in Week 9's win over the Washington Commanders, and head coach Sean McVay has said he is expected to miss "significant time." If he were healthy by Dec. 14, he would not miss any additional time because of the suspension.

Nacua's suspension comes following a roller coaster of an offseason, as he faced a civil lawsuit from a woman over an alleged biting incident and antisemitic comments earlier this year. An attorney representing Nacua denied the woman's claim that he made the antisemitic comment while downplaying the bite mark he left on her.

The Bengals quarterback will not speak on his normal day as he is dealing with an illness, according to the team. If he's feeling better on Friday, he will meet with reporters. #3046. NBA Cup scores, standings, and scenarios: Who can clinch and who's nearly toast?

It's the fourth night of the NBA Cup, and for some teams who want to raise a banner, this might be their last chance to stay in the tournament.

Entering the games on Nov. 20, or "Moving Day," each group leader has two wins, while six teams are looking for their first win, including the reigning NBA Cup champions, the New York Knicks.

The night kicks off with the Charlotte Hornets, currently sporting the league's worst offense, facing the surprising Atlanta Hawks. The nightcap features the Houston Rockets battling the Denver Nuggets, while the Los Angeles Lakers take on the lowly Sacramento Kings, who have the worst record in the Western Conference.

Charlotte Hornets at Atlanta Hawks (East Group C) – 7 ET, NBA League Pass

Orlando Magic at Brooklyn Nets (East Group A) – 7:30 ET, NBA League Pass

Indiana Pacers at Cleveland Cavaliers (East Group B) – 7:30 ET, NBA League Pass

Toronto Raptors at Detroit Pistons (East Group A) – 7:30 ET, Prime

Washington Wizards at Chicago Bulls (East Group C) – 8 ET, NBA League Pass

New Orleans Pelicans at Oklahoma City Thunder (West Group B) – 8 ET, NBA League Pass

Phoenix Suns at Utah Jazz (West Group A) – 9:30 ET, NBA League Pass

Houston Rockets at Denver Nuggets (West Group A) – 10 ET, Prime

Sacramento Kings at Los Angeles Lakers (West Group C) – 10 ET, NBA League Pass

Orlando is the only team in action on Nov. 20 that can reach 3-0 by beating the Brooklyn Nets. Miami and Boston are also undefeated, but they don't play their third Group games until next week.

The Hornets, Jazz, Kings, and Pelicans need to win their first NBA Cup to stay in contention, while the Nets will be eliminated if they lose and Detroit wins. Milwaukee, whose next Group game is Nov. 25, will exit stage left if both Toronto and Orlando win.

#3037. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.

#3032. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#3029. Graves leaning on Raptors motto ‘Stronger Together’ amid losing streak

The Toronto Raptors are in a difficult spot.

While it may (or may not) be anything, there's an uneasy dread that surrounds Kawhi Leonard's injury timeline. Many fans anticipated going into the season understanding that Leonard's health would always loom over the team, but no one could have expected the string of injuries that have plagued the rest of the roster.

With RJ Barrett out for at least two weeks (hamstring), Scottie Barnes managing a groin injury (but expected to return), and Collin Murray-Boyles in concussion protocol, it's been a challenging start to the 2026-27 season.

And it doesn't get easier when the Raptors play the Detroit Pistons on Friday.

Here are two players to watch ahead of the 7:30 p.m. EST scheduled tip-off on Prime Video.

After playing a career-high 31 minutes against the Washington Wizards, Allen Graves took the podium to discuss the team's sixth consecutive loss. The analytics show why the 20-year-old is an intriguing player, but hearing him speak, it's always easy to see why the front office was enamoured with Graves the person.

Graves referred to one of the team's mottos, which can be found inside the OVO Athletic Centre: Stronger Together

"We're really taking emphasis on that and pulling together throughout this time because we've got each other," said Graves. "Trying to pull together so we can get out of this."

While the fix doesn't begin with Graves, the rookie plays an integral role in finding a solution to the team's woes, particularly with Murray-Boyles navigating a head injury.

After shooting 41.3 per cent on 2.6 attempts at Santa Clara, the shooting is finally starting to come around for Graves. He's still only averaging 28.1 per cent on 2.1 attempts through 15 games, but Graves is also confidently shooting 37.5 per cent on 2.6 attempts in the last six contests.

Highly regarded for his quick hands and activity on defence, Graves is taking the journey that every rookie goes on as they navigate the pro game's speed and officiating. While Graves had one steal and three blocks in the home opener against the Chicago Bulls on Oct. 21, he hasn't finished with at least 2+ stocks since. The only other time Graves has finished with at least one block and a steal was against the Minnesota Timberwolves on Oct. 23.

"Getting more comfortable playing on the ball and off the ball," said Graves. "Learning how to guard pin downs and guard screens."

Graves is a naturally gifted rebounder and it's only a matter of time before his elite timing transfers to his closeouts and decision-making on defence. It certainly accelerates the learning process when playing the New York Knicks in a doubleheader followed by the Pistons.

"You can tell they were a championship team," said Graves. "When they make mistakes, getting right back to it, next-play mentality, I feel like that's something we're growing in."

A breakout is happening in Detroit and it's being authored by one of the most intriguing players in the NBA – Ausar Thompson.

The do-it-all wing is averaging 10.4 points, 8.5 rebounds, 5.5 assists, 1.7 steals and 0.8 blocks in his fourth year in the league. Thompson's points, rebounds, and assists are all career-high averages. The fun part is that he's doing it while playing just three more minutes a game.

For those who play Yahoo Fantasy Basketball, Thompson likely made a tremendous impact in both category and points leagues over the last seven days.

vs. New Orleans: 10.0 points, 7.0 rebounds, 4.0 assists, 2.0 steals

vs. San Antonio: 14.0 points, 9.0 rebounds, 12.0 assists, 5.0 steals

vs. Miami: 10.0 points, 8.0 rebounds, 5.0 assists, 2.0 steals, 2.0 blocks

vs. Washington: 17.0 points, 11.0 rebounds, 11.0 assists, 3.0 steals, 3.0 blocks

Teams continue to famously scheme to avoid Thompson like he's a blanket cornerback, but now his numbers and usage are starting to reflect his sky-high potential.

#3024. Federal Reserve issues FOMC statement

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#3021. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for steady income

Stock markets remain volatile as investors weigh uncertainty in the Middle East, rising interest rates and concerns about the artificial intelligence (AI) trade.

Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking a stable income stream can enhance their portfolios with the addition of dividend stocks. In this regard, tracking recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks that are backed by strong financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is the first dividend pick. Earlier this month, the company paid a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share. At an annualized dividend of $7.12 per share, CVX offers a dividend yield of about 3.5%.

After a meeting with senior leadership, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on CVX and raised his price target to $240 from $225. Among the key takeaways Mehta emphasized was that Chevron is building a solid pipeline of promising international exploration projects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa and the Eastern Mediterranean. In particular, he mentioned the focus on Venezuela, where Chevron expects to more than double gross production to 600 kbd from about 280 kbd by 2031, using three joint ventures.

Mehta also noted Chevron's efforts to deploy new technologies, especially in shale and tight-oil operations. CVX is focused on boosting productivity and efficiency to maximize free cash flow, he added.

Initiatives such as artificial lift optimization, AI and machine learning, and advanced chemical treatments are expected to improve recovery by about 10% in new wells. Consequently, capital spending per barrel in U.S. shale is projected to fall 25% in 2026, with total Permian spending dropping below $3.5 billion.

Mehta also said that Chevron sees power as a growth opportunity and is using its U.S. natural gas portfolio, key equipment and partnerships to meet rising demand. Notably, Chevron recently signed a 20-year power purchase deal with Microsoft (MSFT) to supply 2.67 GW of behind-the-meter capacity. This project, called Kilby, is expected to see its first power delivery in 2028 and generate mid-teens returns and long-term cash flows.

Mehta ranks No. 536 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 11.3%. See Chevron Statistics on TipRanks.

Enterprise Products Partners (EPD) is a master limited partnership (MLP) that provides midstream energy services to producers and consumers of natural gas, natural gas liquids (NGLs), crude oil, refined products and petrochemicals. With a quarterly cash distribution of 56 cents per common unit ($2.24 per common unit annualized), EPD offers a yield of about 6%.

Following a recent call with the company, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on EPD with a price target of $42. The analyst slightly lowered her estimates for the second half of 2026, citing margin and volume normalization, though demand dynamics remain strong.

While the 5-star analyst expects a sequential decline due to normal seasonality and normalization following high spreads in the first half of the year, she still expects EPD to report another solid quarter (Q3 2026) and enter 2027 with a favorable setup. Ahead of Q3 earnings, Scotto expects investors to focus on export demand durability, EPD's ability to profit from spot cargo sales and growth in Permian volumes.

Scotto continues to expect $150 million of common unit buybacks per quarter in the second half of 2026. She expects buybacks to increase to $200 million per quarter in 2027, as capex spending declines and free cash flow improves.

In total, Scotto is bullish on EPD and views it as a core MLP holding that has both offensive and defensive characteristics. The analyst praised EPD's diverse asset base as ensuring stable cash flows. "Furthermore, the partnership's multi-year organic growth backlog helps provide visibility on long-term distribution growth," Scotto said.

Scotto ranks No. 199 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.9%. See Enterprise Products Partners Financials on TipRanks.

Brookfield Infrastructure Partners (BIP) operates a diversified portfolio of high-quality utilities, transport, midstream and data assets. The company declared a quarterly distribution of 45.5 cents per unit, payable on September 29. At an annualized distribution of $1.82, BIP offers a yield of 5.2%.

Recently, BMO Capital analyst Devin Dodge reaffirmed a buy rating on Brookfield Infrastructure with a price target of $47. The analyst cited improved visibility into BIP's growth and views its corporate simplification as a potential catalyst.

"Layering in the benefits from the proposed corporate simplification, a multiple well below historical levels and an attractive yield, we believe BIP offers a compelling risk/reward and it remains a preferred idea," said Dodge.

With more organic growth projects in the pipeline and new partnership investment opportunities, Dodge believes that BIP has clearer visibility into where it will deploy capital. That should support continued double-digit growth in funds from operations (FFO) per unit.

Dodge said the semiconductor foundries that BIP is developing with Intel (INTC) are expected to be fully commissioned by the end of 2026, with returns improving through Q4 2026. Dodge expects the joint venture to boost FFO by 300-400 basis points.

Dodge ranks No. 476 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 68% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.5%. See Brookfield Infrastructure Partners KPIs on TipRanks.

#3015. Game Preview: Nets look to snap losing streak against Magic in NBA Cup

The team hosted Julius Erving and some of the ABA championship teams. On live television, Dr. J innocently said he "fully anticipated the Nets to be so fired up having us here" as the team lost by 30. Veteran leader Julius Randle said it was one of the worst games he's been part of in his 13-year career. Head coach Jordi Fernandez continues to say effort is an issue.

When you lose as much as the Brooklyn Nets have, it all feels like one giant losing streak. To be precise, the Nets have lost three straight games by double figures, allowing at least 120 points in each.

The Orlando Magic also enter Friday looking to bounce back. They surrendered a 17-point lead in a 121-116 loss to the Philadelphia 76ers on Wednesday. Orlando will take a win any way it can get one, especially with Paolo Banchero sidelined by an ankle injury.

Who: Brooklyn Nets (3-11) vs. Orlando Magic (6-8)When: Friday, November 20, 7:30 p.m. ETWhere: Barclays Center, Brooklyn, NYTV/Streaming: YES Network, DAZN

Hard to expect much from the home team. Whether you like Julius Randle or agree with his approach is entirely subjective, but he was right to rip into the team. Someone ought to. He isn't innocent in all this, certainly not while finishing an entire game with zero rebounds.

Either way, maybe this will light a fire under their you-know-whats.

At 3-11, Brooklyn has reached the point where something needs to change. Win a couple games and you're back in the play-in picture. Maybe some of the noise calms down, but right now it's deafening.

As far as Friday is concerned, the Magic have been better than the Nets, but they are not unbeatable — especially without Paolo Banchero. They're looking for wins any way they can get in a buzzsaw of an Eastern Conference.

They enter this game as the eight seed with a 6-8 record. Franz Wagner had 27 points, eight rebounds, and 11 assists, while Jalen Suggs added 18 points and eight rebounds.

"(We're) headed back out on the road," said Magic guard Jalen Suggs. "This (loss) didn't kill no momentum, but it was a tough one to drop."

It's a get-back game for both. Let's see who can actually execute.

Franz Wagner is coming off one of his best all-around performances of the season, and the Nets will have their hands full trying to slow him down.

It continues his onslaught of late. He's now coming off three consecutive games with at least 27 points, and his ability to create for himself and others makes him the engine of the Magic offense.

Friday also brings a personal storyline. Wagner will face his older brother, Moritz, for the first time as opponents after the latter spent the first five seasons of Franz's NBA career as his teammate in Orlando before signing with Brooklyn this summer.

"It's definitely going to be a little weird," Franz said. "We've obviously played in practice against each other. But I look at it as a huge blessing to be able to share the court together at this level. I'm really happy for him in the situation that he's in. I think me, Moe and the rest of the family will really enjoy the game as much as anything."

Oh yeah and it's an NBA Cup Game. We'll see if that means anything.

#3003. Average Four Factor Index Power Rankings: 2026-2027 NBA Season Week 5

After another week of play (which has amounted to 3-4 additional games for most teams), the young season is rounding into form, and the aFFI rankings are coming into sharper focus. In particular, the data are beginning to suggest the emergence of clear tiers of contention, though there are also a handful of teams that are moving rapidly up or down the list. Let's dig in to the numbers!

Regarding the Silver and Black, the last week has seen some exceptional performances (e.g., against Golden State) balance against statistically unremarkable wins and one clunky loss to Detroit. Figure 1 (below) shows that this combination has resulted in the Spurs staying put in fourth place, though the margin separating the top five positions is razor thin (more on that later). San Antonio's overall aFFI value continues to be weighed down by an ∆EFG% value (54.72) that – while above average – is well below almost any other team that is considered a legitimate contender. Even so, the Spurs are doing quite well with respect to the other three differentials, with particularly excellent average percentiles in ∆ORB% (65.81) and ∆FTM/FGA (66). In summary, the Spurs excellent start to the season has leaned heavily on their ability to protect the ball, rebound, and get to the free throw line relative to their opponents.

Stepping back to review the league as a whole, one striking finding in Figure 1 is that there are a number of teams that have risen or fallen rapidly in the last week. Here are some exceptional standouts:

Memphis Grizzlies (↑8) – After Memphis started the season 6-0, they went on a three-game losing streak and seemed destined to be a promising young team that was ultimately unready to become a true contender. However, they have been on an absolute tear recently, riding exceptional effective field goal differentials (their average ∆EFG% percentile of 70.87 is by far the best in the league) to the top of the aFFI rankings. The Grizzlies continue to have some serious flaws (e.g., a dreadful average ∆TOV% percentile), but they are undoubtedly a team to watch.

Miami Heat (↑5) – Miami's 0-4 start was cringeworthy, and they have a long way to go to join this season's truly elite teams. Having said that, their current six-game win streak has included some impressive wins, and their aFFI value has risen above league average due to some excellent average ∆TOV% (60.24) and ∆FTM/FGA (61.58) percentiles.

Golden State Warriors (↓9) – The young season has already been quite a roller coaster ride for the Warriors. They started slow, but went on a hot streak driven in part by some exceptional performances by rookie Yaxel Lendeborg. Unfortunately, that magic has not continued with Steph Curry out of the lineup, and Golden State is currently on a five-game skid that includes four blowout losses.

Brooklyn Nets (↓8) – Let me be clear here: The Nets' aFFI value was never good; however, one week ago it would have been fair to say that Brooklyn was playing like a team that should win about 40% of it's games, even though they were actually winning about half as often. The games played since then suggest that this lukewarm initial assessment was likely a statistical quirk.

Looking at Figure 2 (below), we can see that teams are beginning to separate into distinct aFFI groupings, which provides us the opportunity to classify teams into tiers. At present, I see four tiers, though as time goes on teams that number is likely to rise:

Early Contenders (8 teams) – At present, there are eight teams that have an aFFI value of 56.28 or more, with the 9th-ranked Knicks (53.61) trailing the early contenders by a healthy margin of over 2.5 points. The first month of play suggests that any of these teams could emerge as true championship contenders as the season goes on, though if we're being honest this group could probably be subdivided further. In particular, the top five teams (Memphis, Minneapolis, Houston, San Antonio, and Boston) are noticeably ahead of everyone else and very close to each other, with the aFFI gap between the first-ranked Grizzlies and fifth-ranked Celtics (1.44 points) being just a bit larger than the gap between Boston and the sixth-ranked Lakers (1.21 points).

Likely Playoff/Play-in Participants (8 teams) – This is an interesting group for several reasons. First, it includes teams that are clearly heading in different directions. In particular, Dallas and Golden State appear to be at very high risk of falling to the tier below, whereas nearly every other member of this tier has seen their aFFI ranking rise in the last week. Philly remains by far the most interesting team in this set, as their stellar 13-3 record remains far out of step with a much more modest aFFI value (51.9). The 76ers' record will almost certainly move to 14-3 after they play the woeful Bucks tomorrow. However, after that they'll enter a difficult stretch of games that should reveal a lot about their true status.

Downslope to the Abyss (12 teams) – This is most crowded tier, with a correspondingly varied collection of outlooks. However, based on the first month of play, the best that most of these teams can hope for is a spot in the play-in (an opportunity that some of them will undoubtedly get, especially in the East). Within this group, the most interesting is clearly Atlanta, as its current record (9-6) is much more competitive that its 18th-ranked aFFI value (47.63) would suggest. Whether or not the Hawks can continue to beat the odds will be of particular interest to the Spurs, who are owed Atlanta's pick in the 2027 draft.

Kings' Corner (2 teams) – The Sacramento Kings and Milwaukee Bucks have collectively played 30 games, yet possess just two wins between them (one for each team). With their season over before the end of November, Bucks fans are already turning toward implausible draft and trade scenarios that will rescue them from obscurity. In stark contrast, Kings fans forgot long ago that any other state of being was possible.

Data Source: The underlying data used for this analysis was collected from Basketball Reference.

#3001. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2999. Why wild 49ers anomaly ought to put fright into Vikings fans

The 49ers have seemingly been hit with injuries more than any other NFL team in recent years.

So it's notable any time they have a relatively clean bill of health.

Dre Greenlaw was scheduled to practice Friday. Getty Images

That was the case on Friday as Kyle Shanahan's team had something extremely rare happen: All 53 players on the active roster participated in practice ahead of their key game against the Vikings, with the winner earning the No. 1 seed in the NFC through the first 11 weeks.

That includes star receiver Mike Evans, who is officially listed as questionable for Sunday night's key game in Mexico City after missing the last three contests with Achilles tendinitis.

Having all active players at practice doesn't mean the 49ers are completely healthy, but it's a step in the right direction. They didn't rule any players out for the Vikings game on Friday.

The team is still listing Evans, linebacker Dre Greenlaw (neck), defensive tackle C.J. West (knee), cornerback Nate Hobbs (adductor), cornerback Upton Stout (shoulder, neck), pass rusher Khalil Kareem (ankle), backup quarterback Mac Jones (illness) and linebacker Luke Gifford (chest) as questionable.

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan looks on during the second quarter against the Washington Commanders at Levi's Stadium in Santa Clara, California, Monday, Oct. 19, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

There remains some uncertainty regarding West and Hobbs, who remain on injured reserve. They could be activated before the game. The 21-day practice window for Hobbs closes on Wednesday, while West has until Dec. 2.

Additionally, the 49ers have three of their eight return designations from injured reserve available. Candidates for those include rookie receiver De'Zhaun Stribling, second-year linebacker Nick Martin, running back Isaac Guerendo and pass rusher Sam Okuayinonu.

#2997. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2993. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2991. NBA trade rumors 2026-27: Dejounte Murray update (or, non-update), Kyrie Irving, Trey Murphy, more

Don't expect any NBA trades before Dec. 15, when many of the players who signed new contracts or extensions this summer become eligible to be traded. Even then, it's more likely we'll get closer to the February trade deadline before we see any notable names moved. That includes the guy in the sub-headline below.

While the insatiable NBA rumor mill keeps trying to find clickable updates on Dejounte Murray trades — "The Pelicans talked to the Suns!!!" — the reality is there is no traction toward any kind of deal for the New Orleans guard. Grant Liffman and Chris Mannix break it down well in the video above, but here is the latest.

• Murray, through his agents at Klutch Sports, made a trade request, and the Pelicans publicly acknowledged that, saying, "We will evaluate potential trade opportunities that are in the best interest of our organization." New Orleans hasn't found that yet. Meanwhile, Murray's body language on the court betrays how unhappy he is, and he is shooting 29.6% from 3-point range this season, which doesn't help his trade value.

• To be blunt, teams around the league view Murray as a negative value contract. Those are hard to trade. The fact he is making $32.8 million this season and has a $30.8 million player option for next season also gives teams pause. He is viewed as a 30-year-old guard who's shot 41.9% overall and 30% from 3 in the last two-plus seasons.

• While Murray's camp seems to continue to push the Suns as a destination, league sources have told NBC Sports the Suns have minimal interest, unless the Pelicans want to attach sweetener, such as a first-round pick. That's been reported a lot of other places as well, it's no secret. While the Suns could use a point guard and have done background work on Murray (according to Brian Windhorst of ESPN), they aren't sold on Murray being the All-Star player he was five years ago, and he hasn't looked the same coming off his torn Achilles. How much interest the Pelicans have in Jalen Green should be another question.

• When teams do call the Pelicans, they are more likely to ask about Trey Murphy III and Herb Jones. But Joe Dumars is keeping the price ridiculously high — the Pelicans turned down a trade that would have netted them at least two first-round picks for Murphy over the summer, Michael Scotto of Hoopshype reports.

• Jake Fischer of The Stein Line reports the Suns are more interested in adding a big man with Mark Williams out for the season.

• Utah is one of the teams giving "internal consideration" a potential Kyrie Irving trade, if (or, more likely when) the Mavericks start listening to offers, reports Ian Begley of SNY.TV.

It's worth noting that teams have internal discussions about a lot of players and deals that they never even bother to reach out to the other team about in a serious manner, and where Irving fits in the young timeline in Utah is a good question, but the rumor is out there.

• Multiple reports have coach Brian Keefe's seat in Washington getting hot. This was a team that made moves for veterans in Anthony Davis (playing well) and Trae Young (not playing well) to try and win now, and with the team off to a 5-11 start, someone may be the scapegoat. Washington should have just pivoted to building around AJ Dybantsa, but they are going to be stuck with that horrible four-year, $212 million extension they gave Young this summer for a while. That is the most untradable contract in the league right now.

• Multiple reports have teams interested in trading for Bulls guard Tre Jones. Here is what Zach Lowe said on his Ringer podcast: "Teams are already calling the Bulls about Tre Jones... if he's not the best backup point guard in the NBA, he's very close. If they trade him, they're gonna get real stuff."
